Removed words are refused by `as64` with its ordinary parse error for a word the grammar does not have (`expected one of ...`), not a special message. *Hand-written kernels:* `k_sgemm_nt.tile.s`, `k_sgemm_nt.unit.s` and `k_hist.vec.s` (the gather; it also needed `vaddx`, which was never in the grammar) were deleted, `k_sgemm_nt.ukr.s` uses `vlen.b`, and `k_q8dot`, `k_q4dot` and `k_sad` use `vdacc.b` and `vsad.bu`: on sim64 (`-V`, vector instructions executed) `k_q8dot` 252 to 96 for 1,000 elements (63 strips of 16 to 32 strips of 32, 4 to 3 instructions), `k_q4dot` 352 to 144 for 500 bytes of weights (32 strips of 11 to 16 strips of 9), `k_sad` 160 to 120 for ten rows of 100 bytes (4 to 3 a strip). *Not done:* the LLVM patterns (the pass still spells `vacc`/`vdot` and never emitted a removed word; the compiler could select, with a combine: `vavg` from the (a+b+1)>>1 idiom, `vmulh` from the high half, `vdot.w` from the i16 dot loop, `vdacc` from a partial reduction of a widening multiply, `vmla` v by v from mul+add, the rounding store from add, shift, clamp; the others (`vmulhr`, `vsarr`, `vtbl`, `vsad`) are assembly or intrinsics); wave 2; the benchmarks; the silicon figures of `vec-silicon.md` (not changed here: the memory form of `vtbl` was not synthesised, only the equal-sized `vtbl_one`).

171. **DECISION (the author's request, 2026-11-18): wave 2 of the revised vector family is implemented: the lane compares `vceq vcgt vcgtu`, the bitwise select `vbsl`, and the conversions `vcvt.l.f vcvt.lu.f vcvt.f.l vcvt.f.lu`; 16 words, 347 to 363 words of the family, 58 to 63 base mnemonics.** *The request:* wave 2 as proposed in `docs/encoding-work/vec-survey.md` 5.1 items 10 and 11, with the silicon of `vec-silicon.md` (compare and select 3,801 NAND2 equivalents; the conversions 15,847, to be built only with the floating-point lanes, which the design has: kept as a separate list `CVT` in `vecfam.py`, so that the four words can be dropped without touching the others). *Added (16 words):* `vceq.b .w .l .f`, `vcgt.b .w .l .f`, `vcgtu.b .w .l` (11), `vbsl` (1), `vcvt.l.f`, `vcvt.lu.f`, `vcvt.f.l`, `vcvt.f.lu` (4: the survey listed two; see (3)). *Decided by the owner's criteria where the survey left it open* (each in `vec-isa.md` section 7): **(1)** the compares have the operand forms of the lane operations (vector, scalar register, immediate: a compare against a constant is the common case; no silicon, three more leaves) and write an **integer lane of the size compared, also for `.f`** (a mask is bits: `vand`, `vst.l`, `vbsl` take it; the survey said only "a lane mask"); the floating-point compares are ordered and quiet (a NaN compares false, also equal to itself; `-0.0 == +0.0`); no flag is set; **(2)** `vbsl vD, vM, vA, vB` has no lane suffix (one word, as the survey counted), the mask is the first source and selects `vA` where its bit is 1, `vD` may be `vM`, `vA` or `vB`; the assembler requires the three to hold lanes of the same size; **(3)** the names are `vcvt.<from>.<to>` (the order of `vld.bu.w`, `vst.w.b`, `vacc.<src>.<acc>` and the scalar `FCVTSL`), so `vcvt.l.f` is int32 to float and `vcvt.f.l` float to int32, and the unsigned forms `lu` were added to the survey's two (the request names signed and unsigned; `uitofp` and `fptoui` are as common as the signed ones in quantisation), no double forms (the survey lists none); **(4)** **float to integer truncates toward zero and saturates, a NaN gives the largest value, as the scalar `FCVTSL`** (and `FCVTDQ`, `FCVTDQU`), **not "round to nearest even" as survey item 11 says**: a vector lane and the scalar instruction agree, a C cast and `fptosi` are truncations, the ISA's rule is that a rounding is a different opcode (`FRINT`); the unsigned form gives 0 for a value that truncates below zero and `0xffffffff` for a NaN or `2^32` and more (as `FCVTDQU`); int to float rounds to nearest even (`FCVTLS`); the vector words set no flag (the lane operations never touch `FPSR`, the scalar ones set the invalid flag); a loop that needs rounding to nearest (`quantize_row_q8_0` uses `roundf`) needs a vector rounding word that is not in the family (named as a gap); **(5)** nothing keeps state across a strip and none touches memory or a scalar register, so a fault cannot occur inside them and the restart rule is unchanged. *Done:* `docs/codegen-data/encoding/vecfam.py` (lists `CMP`, `BSL`, `CVT`; one new form `VBSL`, four vector registers; `VOP.VV` takes `vcvt`; the compares are in `LANEOPS`), `tools/encopt/addforms.py` (no `--drop`), the grammar (`spec/pdp64/pdp64.hbnf`: 180 forms and 238 leaves became 181 and 239, one form added, `VBSL`; the leaves of `VOP.VR`, `VOP.VI` and the forms after them moved by one pattern: 911 of the 65,536 first parcels are illegal, was 912; kraft 0.9861; `addforms.py` rewrote the hand-edited comments and the encoding name of the two grammars, which were put back by hand: only the vector lines are new), `spec/pdp64-enc.hbnf` (mnemonic numbers), `tools/mkenc.py --md`, `tools/check-grammar-enc.sh --update`, `docs/codegen-data/encoding/mk_vectors.py` (the golden vectors and the hand-held words did not change: no leaf outside the family moved); `common/pdp64exec_vec.inc` (copied to QEMU by `qemu/sync.sh`); `as64/as64.c` `vec_typecheck` (a compare writes integer lanes of the size compared; `vcvt` changes the lane kind; `vbsl` checks the lane sizes); `tools/manual/isa_vec.py` (entries `VCMP`, `VBSL`, `VCVT`, held (P)); `tools/gen-llvm-td.py` (the word filter now lists the five bases, `llvm/target/PDP64/gen/PDP64GenLayout.h` and `PDP64GenMnemonics.td` +16 words each) and `llvm/bench/usage.py` (`llvm/vector-usage.md`: 86 of 363 words used, the same 86; the new words have the reason "no pattern yet"); `docs/encoding-work/vec-isa.md` section 7; tests `tests/sim/vec-wave2.s` (generated by `tests/sim/gen_vec_wave2.py`: 45 tests against an independent model in Python, the expected values embedded as data, 77 elements so the last strip is short; all 45 were checked to fail when one expected byte is changed) and 14 in `tests/asm-err` (the lane-type checker of the compares, `vbsl` and `vcvt`, and three words the grammar does not have: `vceq.q`, `vcgtu.f`, `vcvt.q.d`). *Hand-written kernels:* none of the existing kernels of `arch-data/vec-forms/` had a compare or select idiom (the clamps are `vmin`/`vmax`, one instruction; `k_blend` is arithmetic): none changed. New kernels in `games/` with the form of wave 1 beside each (`.vecw1.s`; vector instructions executed on sim64 with `-V`, the outputs of the two forms equal): `k_thresh` (`x > t ? a : b`, 16-bit lanes, 100 elements, 7 strips) 63 to 42 (9 to 6 a strip; the wave-1 form is `(t - x) >> 15` as the mask and `b ^ ((a ^ b) & m)`, right only while `t - x` fits 16 bits); `k_maskblend` (`m ? a : b`, bytes, 100 elements, 4 strips) 28 to 20 (7 to 5 a strip); `k_quant` (`clamp(trunc(x * id), -128, 127)`, 100 floats, 13 strips) 52 vector instructions (4 a strip), against a scalar loop of 908 instructions executed (`k_quant.scalar.s`). *Not done:* the LLVM patterns (`icmp`/`fcmp` of vectors, `select` of a vector mask, `sitofp`, `uitofp`, `fptosi`, `fptoui`: another task), a vector rounding word, predication, the less-than and not-equal compares, double and 16-bit conversions, the 64-bit compares; the silicon was not measured again (the figures of `vec-silicon.md`; `vbsl` four read ports and the unsigned conversions were not synthesised: the measured `vcmp_mw` and `cvt_both8` are the survey's two words).
172. **DECISION AND TWO BUGS FOUND (the assistant's, 2026-11-18; accepted by the author the same day: "hidden state is ok if we have precise exceptions and clean restarts"; from the new fault-and-restart test; the owner's standing priority on restarts and precise exceptions, errata 161): the accumulators of a block are named for that block only, and the restart of a hidden block keeps the naming of its accumulators in the hardware's copy made at the `vec`.** *The test:* `tests/sim/vec-restart.S` (generated by `tests/sim/gen_vec_restart.py`; 52 kernels, 729 scenarios; the expected values from an independent Python model, the reference run of each kernel on pages that are all present is in the same program) runs a block of every word of wave 1 and wave 2 (and the control reductions `vacc`, `vmaxacc`, `vminacc`, `vadd`) over a region whose page boundary is in the middle of a strip, with the fault on a load stream (A, B, C and the table of `vtbl.b`), on the store stream, and on a misaligned pointer, in the first strip, a middle strip, the last (short) strip and in a block of one element; the handler records EPC, ECAUSE, EVA, the pointer registers and the scalar register, takes a copy of the destination and maps the page; then the test checks (a) the destination holds no store of the faulting strip (and holds those of the strips before it), (b) the pointers and the scalar are those of the start of the strip (of the entry for a block with hidden accumulation), (c) EPC is the `vec` instruction and EVA the first byte of the page that faulted, (d) after `rti` the memory, the pointers and the scalar equal the reference run and the model. A handler variant overwrites v0 to v15 with garbage by other vec blocks (one of them names accumulators), which stands for a context switch that does not save the hidden registers. On `sim64` the pages are made to fault by the new test-only option `-x LO:HI` (the first access to each page of the window faults once: a load fault or a store fault; the stand-in for "the handler maps the page" in a machine with no MMU); on QEMU the same program builds identity page tables with the window not present, and the handler writes the page-table entry. *Found, with the executor as it was:* (1) **`C->vacc`, the registers that `vclr` named as accumulators, was never cleared**: after the first `vclr` of a program (or after the vector block of a handler that had one) every later block was treated as hidden, so a fault in it restarted the block from its entry instead of at the strip, and its registers were not zeroed at the start of each strip. Restarting from the entry is wrong for any block that writes memory it also reads (`a[i] += 1`: the strips before the fault are done twice) and loses the progress for the others; the rule of `vec-isa.md` 4.1 is that the accumulators belong to the block. *Fix* (`common/pdp64exec_vec.inc`): `vacc` is cleared when a block ends (the registers keep their values: the hand-written micro-kernels name the accumulators with `vclr` before the `vec` and read them after the `vend`). (2) **The restart of a block whose accumulators were named before the `vec` (`vclr.f v0,#8` then `vec`, as `k_sgemm_nt.ukr.s` does) was not exact if the handler ran vector code of its own**: the naming and the (discarded) accumulators of the block are hidden state, the handler's blocks overwrote both, and the restarted block accumulated on the handler's garbage or lost its accumulators. *Fix:* the hidden copy that `vec-isa.md` 4.1 describes ("the hardware keeping a hidden copy of the pointers and registers at `vec`") now holds the naming of the accumulators too: a block with hidden accumulation records the address of its `vec` and the accumulators it names (`vrun_*`); one that is still recorded at the next `vec` was aborted and its record is kept (`vrst_*`) while the handler's own blocks run; the `vec` at the same address restores the naming and zeroes those accumulators. A block whose `vclr` is inside the block (the preamble of the first strip) needs none of this and was already exact. *Not fixed, open:* a handler that names accumulators with a `vclr` outside any block (not followed by a block that ends) leaves the naming set for the interrupted program's next block (the hole of the same kind as (1), but it needs the hardware to clear the naming at `rti`, which is in `pdp64exec.c`; a handler that does this is a handler that uses hidden state, which the operating system is not supposed to do without saving it); an interrupt is never taken inside a block in this executor (the block runs whole, errata 161), so the asynchronous case reduces to the fault case; a bus error (an address with no memory) and a write-protection fault are not in the test (the page fault, store and load, and the misaligned access are). *Mutation check:* the test fails when the executor is changed so that a strip's stores are visible before the fault (check 11), so that the pointers move at each instruction (5), so that `vacc`/`vsad`/`vdacc` write the scalar at once (10), so that the accumulators are not cleared at the end of a block (5), or without the hidden copy (13); each change was made to the file and reverted at once. *Evidence:* sim64 and QEMU run the test (`tools/check-sim64.sh`: 105 run, 0 failed; `tools/check-qemu.sh`: 61 run, 0 failed, 4 skipped).

173. **DECISION (the author's request, 2026-11-18; the details the assistant's): the strip of a vec block is invisible: one binary gives bit-identical results on a 128-bit and a 256-bit implementation. The five places found by the strip study are closed: `vlen` and the register form of `vtbl.b` are removed, the least strip is 16 bytes, and what a fault handler sees is exact (EPC, EVA, ECAUSE) or opaque (the pointers, the scalar result); the floating-point sum (errata 174) and the accumulators (errata 175) are the other two.** *The author's words:* "yes do 2, close the 5 places" (the second of the options offered after `docs/encoding-work/vec-strip.md` 5.1 had listed the places where a program, or the operating system, can see the strip: make the strip invisible, rather than document it as a visible implementation parameter); earlier, on the strip: "the program never names the width" (the author's promise, R471 and R455's criteria); the details were left to the assistant, to be decided by the author's stated priorities (the most work for the fewest instructions; reasonable performance for minimal silicon; safety, security, determinism and reproducibility, precise exceptions, clean restarts; "hidden state is ok if we have precise exceptions and clean restarts"). *The five places* (`vec-strip.md` 5.1) *and the decision for each* (each with its options in `vec-isa.md` section 8): **(1) `vlen.b Rd` returned the bytes of a strip: removed** (the word, the form `VLEN`, its tests, its manual entry, its row in the cycle models; the 363 words of the family stay 363 because `vfin` is added, the base mnemonics stay 63, the forms stay 23 and the prefix grammar 181 forms and 239 leaves). *Options:* keep it and document that a program must not depend on it (a promise the program can break: rejected, the point is that it cannot), make it return a fixed value (a lie that breaks packing: rejected), remove it (chosen: nothing in the family or the compilers needs it; the compiled kernels never used it, `llvm/vector-usage.md`; the one hand kernel that did, `k_sgemm_nt.ukr.s`, now has a fixed tile of 8 rows by 4 columns, 4 columns being 16 bytes of binary32: errata 175). **The least strip is 16 bytes** and every strip is a power of two from 16 bytes up to an implementation maximum; a strip of 8 bytes cannot hold a segment of four 4-byte fields in a beat, the 16 entries of the table of `vtbl.b`, or the 16-byte chunk of the floating-point sum, so it is **outside the architecture**: `vec-strip.md` carries a note (the measurements stand as measurements; the recommendation of 128 bits is the least strip, not an option below it) and `sim64 -S 8` (and `PDP64_VSTRIP=8`) still runs for the cycle study but prints a warning on standard error, and a program that sums floating-point lanes may give another result there. **(2) The register form `vtbl.b vD, vT, vI` took the table from the lanes of `vT`, so a short or a differently wide strip truncated or changed it: removed**; only `vtbl.b vD, (Ra), vI` (16 bytes at `(Ra)`) stays; a table in a register is stored first. *Options:* the survey's group of 16 lanes (strip dependent: the test `vec-wave1` test 63 differed at 8, 16 and 64 bytes), "the table is bytes 0 to 15 of `vT` for every lane" (the study's proposal: the same multiplexer, but a table loaded by `vld` into a register of a 16-byte strip has all its lanes only if the strip is 16 or more, and a block that loads the table in every strip is the memory form with a copy), remove (chosen: one form for one meaning; the memory form was already there and costs the same, `vtbl_one` 12,663 against `vtbl_g16` 12,736 NAND2). **(5) What a fault handler saw mid-block** (the pointers and the scalar result at the start of the faulting strip, a whole number of strips: strip dependent): **EPC, EVA and ECAUSE are exact and strip independent; the pointer registers and the scalar result are opaque** (an element boundary chosen by the implementation, no later than the faulting element, never interpreted by the operating system, which restarts by returning). EPC is the address of the `vec` as before. EVA and ECAUSE are those of the first faulting access in the order of the elements (the least element, then the first instruction of the block, then the first field of a segment; the table, the scalar of `vfmab` and an epilogue belong to the strip's first element): before, the executor found the first fault in the order of the strip (instruction by instruction), so a block whose load of the second stream faulted at element 2 and whose first stream faulted at element 6 reported either, by strip; now the checks of all the accesses of a strip are made before it does any, in the order of the elements (`vprecheck` in `common/pdp64exec_vec.inc`), which also makes the abort precise by construction (nothing done when it is raised). *Options:* leave the pointers as they were and document the strip dependence (rejected: an operating system that adds the done part of a strip to a pointer is wrong at the next width), define the progress exactly (every pointer at the faulting element: rejected, a strip's loads and register results cannot be partly committed without hidden state per lane), opaque (chosen, the contract of `rti`: "restart by returning"). The test that had looked at the strip is now a relation (`vec-isa.md` 8.6): every pointer its entry value plus the same number of elements `e'`, `e'` no later than the faulting element (0 for a block that restarts from its entry), the scalar the model's after exactly `e'` elements, the memory the model before `e'`, the initial bytes from the faulting element on, and either between; rows are added in which two streams fault (the first EVA must be the least element's) and in which the handler reads only EPC, EVA and ECAUSE (the restart must give the run with no fault). *Done:* `common/pdp64exec_vec.inc` (`VLEN` removed, `VO_FIN`, the strip of an accumulator block, `vprecheck`, `vstrip_run`, the table lookup from memory only; the QEMU copies by `qemu/sync.sh`, QEMU rebuilt); `docs/codegen-data/encoding/vecfam.py` (`VLEN` replaced by `VFIN`, `vtbl.b` out of `VOP.VVV`; `tools/encopt/addforms.py --drop VLEN`, `tools/mkenc.py --md`, `tools/check-grammar-enc.sh --update`, `docs/codegen-data/encoding/mk_vectors.py`; **the numbers were compacted again**: `VLEN` was a one-parcel form with a register field (32 first-parcel patterns), `VFIN` has no operand and is a two-parcel form like `VEND` (one pattern), so every leaf after it moved and the expected words of 8 tests of `tests/asm`, `tests/ld/link.words`, the golden vectors and the checksums were written again, and `tools/mkmanual.py` rewrote the manual's figures and dictionary); `as64` (the checks of `vec_typecheck` for the new rules, 8.5); `tools/gen-llvm-td.py` (the list of the family's words: `vlen` out, `vfin` in; `llvm/target/PDP64/gen` regenerated), `tools/manual/isa_vec.py` (entries `VEC`, `VFMA`, `VTBL`, `VACC`, `VCLR` changed, `VLEN` replaced by `VFIN`; all still held (P)), `llvm/bench/usage.py` and `llvm/vector-usage.md`, `sim64` (the warning), `tools/check-sim64.sh` (`NAME.strips`: the strips at which the same program runs again), `tools/strip-equiv.py` (rewritten: the whole suite and `llvm/check.sh` at 16, 32 and 64 with no check switched off, and the probe), tests `tests/sim/vec-strip.s` (new, `gen_vec_strip.py`), `vec-restart.S` (856 rows against 729, 56 kernels against 52), `vec-wave1.s` (the register-form test removed: 72 tests, from 73), `vec-basic.s` (check 4 is now a block with an accumulator and a `vfin`), `tests/asm-err` (4 files new: `removed-vlen-b`, `removed-vtbl-reg`, `fsum-wide-lane`, `fsum-fd-lane`; `vtbl-reg-lane` removed with its form), `llvm/tests/vec/w3_fsum.c` (new). *Evidence:* `tools/check-sim64.sh`: 122 run, 0 failed (105 before: the new test twice, and the five vec tests at the strips 16, 32 and 64); `tools/strip-equiv.py`: the 53 programs of `tests/sim` at strips 16, 32 and 64 give the expected exit status and the same output **with no check switched off** (before: 3 tests failed by design and were patched), the probe is equal to the model at every strip, and `llvm/check.sh` passes at 16, 32 and 64 (61, 5, 21, 5, 21 and 88 vector programs; 84 before, `w3_fsum` added with its four variants). *What a program or the OS may still see* (`vec-isa.md` 8.8): time; the memory a handler sees between the progress and the faulting element; the pointers and the scalar result there (opaque); the strip of 8 bytes, outside the architecture; and a block whose streams overlap, which is unpredictable.

174. **DECISION (the assistant's, under errata 173, 2026-11-18): the floating-point sum has a canonical order that does not depend on the strip: chunks of 16 bytes of source lanes, each by a fixed tree, added to the register one after the other.** *The problem* (the fourth place of `vec-strip.md` 5.1): `vacc.f` over 1000 seeded values gave 0xc602a2a3, 0xc602a2a0, 0xc602a2a1, 0xc602a2a1 at strips 8, 16, 32, 64: a strip-dependent, so a width-dependent, result; for safety, security and reproducibility (the author's criteria) a program must get the same bits on every implementation. *The options, with their silicon (yosys, `tools/gates/vecred.v`, `run-red.sh`, `strip-results/red.tsv`; NAND2 equivalents, a floating-point add 2,331) and their cycles:* **(a) exact accumulation** (a 288-bit fixed-point accumulator, an alignment shifter and a 288-bit adder for each lane, one rounding at the end): associative so independent of any order, and more accurate; 25,858 / 50,683 / 100,306 NAND2 at 128 / 256 / 512 bits plus 13,794 for the rounding plus 288 flip-flops of hidden state (2.7 times the silicon of the tree at 256 bits, 3.8 with the rounding), the same cycles as the tree; rejected on silicon (the author's second priority), kept as the answer if bit-exactness against a reference sum is ever required. **(b) the author's example: G = 8 virtual lanes, a partial for each element modulo 8 summed in element order, combined by a fixed tree at the end** (hidden partials): 37,706 / 36,299 / 54,713 NAND2 (twice the tree at 256 bits, four times at 128), 256 flip-flops of hidden state, and **a fault in the middle of a long sum restarts the block from its entry** (a sum over a megabyte demand-paged repeats up to the whole block at each new page; a program must cut the sum into bounded blocks to bound it); pros: 8 elements for each add latency (twice the chosen order at 256 bits) and no recurrence through the register inside a strip; rejected (silicon, hidden state, a restart cost that grows with the block). **(b') chunks of 16 bytes chained through the register (chosen):** the lanes of the stream in chunks of 16 bytes of source lanes (4 binary32, 2 binary64; a short last chunk has the lanes it has), each chunk by the tree `(l0 + l1) + (l2 + l3)` in the accumulate precision with every addition rounded to nearest even, the chunks added to the register in element order; **9,416 / 18,466 / 36,750 NAND2: the same adders as the old tree** (3 for each chunk and 1 for the register: the count is one adder a lane at every width), no state, **the register is committed with the strip, so a fault restarts at the strip: a fault in the middle of a long sum costs nothing and no restart rule is needed** (tested in `vec-restart`: `vacc.f`, `vacc.f then vst`, `vacc.d`, `vacc.f.d`, faults in the first, a middle and the last strip); its cost is that a strip of `strip/16` chunks chains that many adds through the register: **none at 128 bits** (one chunk a strip), 2 adds a strip at 256 bits and 4 at 512; in model 1 (`-V`) a `vacc.f`/`.d` costs one cycle for each chunk, in model 2 one `fma`-class latency for each chunk after the first; on the benchmark kernels (port 16 bytes a cycle, commit 1; the 5 kernels with a floating-point sum change, the other 42 programs not by a cycle): `k_ddot` 1,283 to 1,533 cycles at strip 32, `k_dgemv_n` 3,025 to 3,537, `k_sdot` 659 to 784, `k_rmsnorm` 7,523 to 7,648, `k_sgemm_nt` 3,474 to 3,986; **no change at strip 16**, little at 64 (the loops are bound by the memory port there). **(c) sequential element order** (one adder; one element for each add latency: 4 to 8 times slower than (b') at 128 and 256 bits, the vector unit gains nothing on a sum): rejected. **(d) leave the order to the implementation and make the assembler refuse a sum in code that wants reproducibility:** does not close the place (the program must then not use the word at all): rejected. *The rule the assembler enforces:* a floating-point sum may not be in a block that has a lane wider than its source lane (`vacc.f`, `vacc.f.d` with a lane wider than 4 bytes, `vacc.d` with none wider than 8 is always fine), because the strip would not be a whole number of chunks: `as64` refuses it with a message (`tests/asm-err/fsum-wide-lane.s`, `fsum-fd-lane.s`), and the LLVM pass (`PDP64VecLoop.cpp`) makes no such block (`llvm/tests/vec/w3_fsum.c`: a float sum is one block, a float sum beside a 64-bit sum is the scalar loop). `vmaxacc` and `vminacc` take the lanes in element order (a comparison is exact; the order shows only in the sign of a zero and a NaN, and it is the stream's: tested with signed zeros at the strip boundaries of 16, 32 and 64 bytes). The integer reductions are exact and unchanged. The sum of `vfma` and `vfmab` into accumulators is errata 175. *Done:* `vop_reduce` in `common/pdp64exec_vec.inc`; the model changes; `as64`'s `vec_typecheck`; the independent model in `gen_vec_strip.py`, `gen_vec_restart.py` and `tools/strip-equiv.py` (exact rational arithmetic rounded to binary32: no double rounding); the values of the 88 vector programs of `llvm/check.sh` did not change (their sums are of small integers, exact in every order). *Evidence:* the probe of `tools/strip-equiv.py`: `vacc.f` 0x4df180fd, `vacc.d` 0x41be301fcd7e4243, `vacc.f.d` 0x41be301fcd7e240f at strips 16, 32 and 64, equal to the model; at the strip of 8 bytes, outside the architecture, 0x4df180fb and the others differ. A copy of the executor with the tree taken over the whole strip fails `vec-strip` at strips 32 and 64 (it passes at 16, where the strip is one chunk): the test is not empty. *Open:* a sum that must be exact (or correctly rounded) needs option (a), which is not built.

175. **DECISION (the assistant's, under errata 173, 2026-11-18): a block that names accumulators (a `vclr`, accumulators named before it, a `vfin` or a `vfmab`) has a strip of exactly 16 bytes on every implementation; the one strip-independent way to read an accumulator out is the epilogue after the new word `vfin`; the micro-kernel has a fixed tile of 8 rows by 4 columns.** *The problem* (the third place, and a sixth that closing it showed): an accumulator register holds lanes of the strip, so reading it inside the block (kernels 48, 49 and 51 of `vec-restart`: a `vst` or a `vacc` of the running accumulator in every strip) showed the partition into strips; floating-point accumulation into an accumulator (`vfma`, `vfmab`) had the order of the lanes of the strip (errata 174's problem, in registers); and `vfmab vD, vA, (Ra)+` moves `Ra` by one element for each strip, so after the block `Ra` counted strips and which scalar met which lane depended on the strip. A GEMM micro-kernel is the one user, and it is a strip-shaped tile by nature (one strip is one k step, the columns are the lanes). *The options:* (i) the assistant's first recommendation: refuse (`vec_typecheck`) every plain read of an accumulator inside the block and give one extraction form; it would have refused 3 of the 56 kernels of the restart test, left floating-point accumulation and the pointer of `vfmab` open, and needed the extraction form anyway; (ii) **a fixed strip for the blocks that have accumulators: 16 bytes, the least strip, the same on every implementation** (chosen): the lanes of an accumulator, the partition of the stream into strips, the pointer of `vfmab` and the order of the floating-point lanes are all the program's, so **every use of an accumulator inside the block is architectural and nothing has to be refused**; the cost is one control rule (the strip of a block that has accumulators or a `vfmab`: 16 bytes) and that such a block on a 256-bit implementation runs at half its width; accumulator blocks are ranked last (no compiled kernel makes one) and the micro-kernel is 8 rows by 4 columns of binary32, 32 multiply-adds a strip against 64 at 256 bits; (iii) a 32-byte architectural tile with a 16-byte machine doing two passes for each instruction: needs a register file of twice the width on the small machine (the 61,578 NAND2 of the registers at 256 bits against 30,869 at 128 are half the silicon of the unit): rejected. *The read-out:* **`vfin`** (a base mnemonic with no operand, a two-parcel form like `vend`): the instructions of the block after it, the epilogue, run once, after the last strip, as one more strip of `VL` lanes (`VL` = 16 bytes over the widest lane of the block) with no stream: `(Ra)+` moves once, `d(Ra)` does not, a load loads `VL` elements, the accumulators hold their final values; a `vacc`, `vmaxacc` or `vst` of an accumulator there happens once on its final value; the epilogue's accesses are checked, stores held and committed as one strip (the abort is precise), and **a fault in the epilogue restarts the whole block from its entry** (hidden state restarts clean: errata 172; tested: `vec-restart` kernel "hidden: vclr.l + vadd.l, vfin, a table load and vacc.l.q" with a fault in the table the epilogue loads). `vfin` makes the block an accumulator block by itself. *Gates:* none measured (a control rule and an extra pass of the strip sequencer); the extra decode is one instruction. *The micro-kernel* (`docs/encoding-work/arch-data/vec-forms/blas/k_sgemm_nt.ukr.s`, run by `tests/sim/vec-strip.s` at every strip): `vclr.f v0,#8`, `vld.f v8,(r1)+`, eight `vfmab.f`, `vfin`, eight times `vld.f v9,(Rc); vadd.f v9,v9,vN; vst.f v9,(Rc)` with the eight C-row pointers (r16 to r23) made before the block; the kernel is 49 instructions against the old one's 51 (the old one spent 5 on `vlen`, the shift and the multiply for the end of the panel and 8 `add`s between the rows; the new one 11 on the end of the panel and the eight row pointers); the old kernel could not be run (its tail read the accumulators outside the block, where no vector instruction but `vclr` is defined) and now it is. *The seventh place* (found, not closable by a definition): a block whose store stream overlaps a load stream at a different element gives a strip-dependent result (the loads of a strip are done before its stores, so a lane that reads what an earlier lane of the same strip stores sees the old value, and what one strip stores the next loads); the text of `vec-isa.md` 4.5, "the result of overlapping streams in different strips is the sequential one", was itself strip dependent and is withdrawn: **overlapping streams are unpredictable** (a program error, as an overlapping `memcpy`); the LLVM pass keeps the scalar loop unless it proves the streams the same element or disjoint (its run-time test). *Evidence:* `tests/sim/vec-strip.s` checks 21 to 23 (the running accumulator read in the block, the `vfma` accumulation with `vfin` and `vacc.f`, the micro-kernel) pass at 16, 32 and 64; the 56 kernels of `vec-restart` pass at all three.

176. **DECISION (the owner's request, 2026-11-18; the details the assistant's): the vec block has a 2D (tile) form: `vec2 Rs, Re, Rn, Rw` with `vskip Ra, Rg` per stream; implemented, tested and measured; the compiler does not emit it.** *The author's words:* "i want to know ... is it possible to set up the instructions once then just keep reading in tiles and rerunning it? ... about the scalar loop; if its running millions of times as the outside loop not the inner loop, its ok; what efficiency gain is there with the 2d block? not code size. execution speed/efficiency? now if it makes it easier to do things more securely without bounds checks... excellent. go ahead and design the 2d block; you sure noone else has done this before? measure and find out why not. and yes, i do want you to rerun the graphics shaped kernels with and without the 2d block form at both strips." *What was built* (full text, options and numbers in `docs/encoding-work/vec-2d.md`): the words `vec2` (form `VBEG2`, four registers, 16 first-parcel patterns) and `vskip` (the free sub-opcode of `vec`); the family 363 to 365 words, 63 to 65 base mnemonics, 23 to 24 forms; the grammar 181 forms and 239 leaves to 182 and 240, 911 to 926 illegal first parcels (the optimiser's layout was run again by the generator workflow of errata 170: `vecfam.py`, `addforms.py`, `mkenc.py --md`, `check-grammar-enc.sh --update`, `mk_vectors.py`; **every encoding moved**: `tests/enc/vectors.txt`, 8 word files of `tests/asm`, `tests/ld/link.words` and the checksums were written again; the generator writes boilerplate (the member name `pdp64/32` and two comment blocks) that the hand-edited grammar does not have, which was put back by hand: a defect of `addforms.py`, not fixed); `common/pdp64exec_vec.inc` (the row loop, `vrow_advance`), `sim64 --vrow=N` and both cycle models, the QEMU copies (`qemu/sync.sh`, qemu rebuilt), `as64` (`vec_typecheck`), `tools/manual/isa_vec.py`, `tools/vec-glance.py`, `tools/gen-llvm-td.py`, `llvm/bench/usage.py`, `vec-isa.md` section 9. *The decisions* (the owner: "decide design details yourself by your stated priorities"): **(1)** the spelling `vec2` and per-stream `vskip` (fewest instructions and patterns; a pitch operand on every memory word would double the forms of the load and store family); **(2)** the pitch of a stream is a *gap* (the pitch minus the bytes of its row; the DSP's post-modify), because a pitch would need each stream's row start, which is hidden state; **(3)** the row counter `Rn`, the row's end `Re` and the width `Rw` are ordinary registers (four registers: `Rs`, `Re`, `Rn`, `Rw`, all different): a restart in mid-row must know the end of the row and the width of the next, so nothing is hidden and a context switch saves nothing extra; **(4)** the row advance is committed with the last strip of its row, so a fault restarts at the faulting strip of the faulting row and no strip is repeated (tested: 117 cases, strips 16, 32, 64, sim64 and QEMU; 913 rows executed for 913 rows in the tiles, with 93 restarts); **(5)** a strip never spans rows (a packed-rows strip is analytic only, 1.3 to 1.7 times on the narrowest tiles, two cache accesses a strip); **(6)** blocks with accumulators are refused in 2D (a restart from the entry would lose the tile); **(7)** at most four `vskip` (four adders); **(8)** no hardware bounds operand: the software idiom, one `chk2` of the first and the last row of each stream, costs 22 cycles (SAD) and 41 (four-stream blend) a tile, 2.0% at 64 x 64, 0.1% at 256 x 256, against about 2 per row and stream in the loop of blocks (equal at about 6 rows); the hardware form is 3,999 NAND2 for four streams (1.4% of the vector unit) and two more registers a stream; **(9)** the row cost in the models is a parameter, default 1 cycle (an assumption; 0 and 2 shown). *Measured* (sim64 models 1 and 2, 4,428 runs, every form writes the bytes of the scalar build): the 2D block over the loop of 1D blocks, strip 16, 16 x 16: SAD 1.92, average 1.95, mask blend 1.75, alpha blend 1.27, 8-tap filter 1.10, gray 1.28, threshold 1.94, quantise 1.26; at 64 x 64: 1.34, 1.32, 1.24, 1.07, 1.03, 1.08, 1.34, 1.07; at 256 x 256 the SAD 1.09 (the loop overhead is under 10% from 16 strips a row, under 5% from about 30); a 256-bit strip gains nothing on rows of 16 bytes or less for byte kernels (1.00; the owner's suspicion confirmed) and 1.4 to 1.9 for kernels that widen; row advance silicon 4,617 NAND2 (0.8% with a shared adder 2,030: 1.7 to 1.9% and 0.8% of the vector unit); a 16 x 16 tile touches 8.03 pages at pitch 1920, 16 at 4096. *The premise "no one has done this" is false in part* (AMX `TILELOADD` with a stride and a start row, Intel Xe 2D block messages, Hopper TMA, SME slices, MOM, AltiVec `dst`, DSP nested loops); the shape here (a loop of the 1D block, progress in ordinary registers) was not found. *Not done:* the compiler pass (18 loops of the benchmark sources contain a loop, all countable, 15 with a vectorised loop inside; a recogniser would take the disjointness proof across rows), a hardware bounds operand, row packing, `vec2` in the one-instruction prefix form, the LLVM rebuild with the new name tables.

177. **FIXED (the assistant's, R490, 2026-11-18): the generator workflow of errata 170 no longer writes stale text into the grammars; the workflow run on the committed tree changes no file (the defect named in errata 176: "`addforms.py` writes boilerplate that the hand-edited grammar does not have, put back by hand").** *Cause:* `tools/encopt/addforms.py` writes the grammars with `docs/codegen-data/encoding/write_hbnf.py`, which holds the text of the first generation: the header comment of `spec/pdp64-enc.hbnf` (the "pdp64/16, pdp64/32" wording, the tile registers `tregW`, the shape titles), the header comment and the member name `pdp64/32` of `spec/pdp64/pdp64.hbnf`. The grammars were edited by hand afterwards (one member, the name `pdp64`, no tile registers), so every run overwrote the hand text. *Fix:* `addforms.py` takes the committed grammars as the text it does not decide: in `pdp64-enc.hbnf` a block of comment lines that the writer words differently is the committed one; in the member file the header comment and the `encoding-name` line are the committed ones, with the counts of the first line (registers, forms, leaves) the new ones; everything the forms decide (forms, leaves, mnemonics, the comments that carry counts) is generated as before. *Proof:* the whole workflow (`vecfam.py`, `addforms.py` without `--drop`, copy to `spec/`, `mkenc.py --md`, `check-grammar-enc.sh --update`, `mk_vectors.py`) was run on the tree with no change to `vecfam.py`, and a comparison of the whole tree (excluding `.git`, `qemu/build`) before and after, by checksum, found no changed file (`spec/`, `tests/enc`, `tests/asm`, `tests/ld`, `qemu/target-pdp64/gen`, `llvm/` included). *The check:* `tools/check-grammar-enc.sh` now runs `addforms.py` into a scratch directory first and requires its two grammars to equal the committed ones byte for byte; it fails, with the diff, when `vecfam.py` was changed without the workflow or the vec family was edited by hand (tried: one mnemonic of a vec leaf altered in a scratch copy, the check failed with that line). Consequence: a hand edit inside the vec family has to be made in `vecfam.py`.

178. **DECISION (the assistant's, R491, 2026-11-18, on the owner's request for the exposure assessment of CVE-2026-99312; accepted by the owner's instruction to decide the details by the stated priorities): the manual states what happens to cached translations when `MMUCTL`, `PTBR` or `ASID` is written and at reset (new 6.8.4 and 6.8.5); QEMU conforms and has a test; sim64 has no translation and cannot test it.** *The prompt:* the owner asked to examine CVE-2026-99312 (the Linux KVM x86 MMU context was not reset when emulating INIT for a nested guest with paging disabled, leaving stale translations) and for the assessment of what pdp64 does. *Exposure:* the pdp64 has no hypervisor, so it is not exposed; but the manual did not state the rule (6.8 left it open, J.6.8, and the entry of `MFSR` said "flushes the translation buffer" against the purpose of the tags). *The rule (6.8.4, 6.8.5, tagged (D)):* **(a)** a write to `MMUCTL` that changes bit 0, either way, discards every cached translation of every `ASID` and the global ones; **(b)** a write to `PTBR` that changes its value discards the global entries and the entries of the current `ASID`, unless the `ASID` is also changed before the next `ISYNC` (then the number is the kernel's binding to one table, a reused number being invalidated with `TLBIA` as 6.8 says); an entry walked from the old table is never used under the new one with the same `ASID`; **(c)** a write to `ASID` promises no flush: an entry of another number is never used (the tag), whether it is kept is the implementation's; **(d)** all take effect at the next `ISYNC` (6.5.2); **(e)** reset leaves no cached translation, and any event that resets a core's or a guest's translation state must discard its cached translations (stated in 6.8.5, and for future virtualisation in B.2.11.2 and J.2.3). *Why:* safety and security first, no new hardware: (a) costs nothing (a rare event) and closes the stale-entry window at the moment translation changes meaning; (b) costs a compare of the ASID at the `ISYNC` and keeps the context switch of 6.11 free of flushes (the new `ASID` comes with the new table); the alternative of flushing on every `PTBR` write would have made the tags dead weight. J.6.8 is decided except for an invalidate by `ASID`, which stays open. *Implementations:* the executor (`common/pdp64exec.c`, shared by QEMU) flushes everything on a write to `MMUCTL`, `PTBR`, `ASID` and `MTASID`, and at `ISYNC`: stricter than the rule, so it conforms, and no code was changed. sim64 has no MMU and no translation buffer (`h_tlb` does nothing, translation is always off): the rule cannot be checked there, there is no sim64 test, and none is claimed. *The test:* `tests/qemu/mmu-flush.S` (run by `tools/check-qemu.sh` on the kernel-mode machine only): (i) P cached, translation off, the entry changed behind the buffer, translation on: P reads the new page, twice; (ii) the same through a write to `PTBR` (and back); (iii) the same through a write to `ASID` (two switches). *Non-vacuity:* with a scratch edit of `qemu-src/target/pdp64/gen/pdp64exec.c` (outside the repository; reverted by `qemu/install.sh` and rebuilt), the flushes selected by an environment variable: with the flush removed from both the write and the `ISYNC` the test failed at check 2 for `MMUCTL`, 4 for `PTBR`, 6 for `ASID`, 2 for all; with only the write or only the `ISYNC` removed it passed, because the emulator flushes at both (the test checks the pair as the architecture promises it, not either alone). With the edit reverted it passes. *Not tested:* reset (the harness has no way to reset the machine, `-monitor none` and no guest reset device; from the source of QEMU v11.1.2: `cpu_common_reset_hold` calls `cpu_exec_reset_hold`, which calls the TCG hook `tcg_cpu_reset_hold` (`accel/tcg/tcg-accel-ops.c`): `tcg_flush_jmp_cache(cpu); tlb_flush(cpu);`, and `pdp64_cpu_reset_hold` calls the parent's hold first and then zeroes the registers, so reset flushes QEMU's soft TLB and leaves `MMUCTL` zero); the retention of other-`ASID` entries (not promised); the global bit; the broadcast of `TLBI`; real silicon (there is none).

179. **DECISION (the owner's rule, 2026-11-18; the details the assistant's, R492): a generator owns whole files and never writes a file a person edits: every generated hbnf is an include of a hand-edited master.** *The owner's words:* "any generated hbnf should be an include into the master hbnf file that is hand edited." *Why:* the grammar is the source of truth, yet generated and hand-edited text were mixed in the same files (`spec/pdp64/pdp64.hbnf`, `spec/pdp64-enc.hbnf`), so every regeneration overwrote hand-written text; errata 177 patched `tools/encopt/addforms.py` with `keep_hand_text()` to put the committed header, the name and the shape titles back. *The assistant's decisions (by the owner's stated priority, the grammar as the one source, and the least surprise):* **(1)** `spec/generated/` holds three whole generated files, each beginning `; GENERATED by tools/encopt/addforms.py (through docs/codegen-data/encoding/write_hbnf.py) from the forms of the encoding: do not edit.`: `pdp64-forms.hbnf` (`insn`, the catalogue `forms`, every `form-`, `leaf-` and `sub-` rule: the prefix code of the 182 forms, 240 leaves; the vec family of `vecfam.py` is in it), `pdp64-shapes.hbnf` (the operand shapes and `noop`) and `pdp64-ext.hbnf` (`mn-ext`, with the aliases). **(2)** The masters stay hand edited and keep every human line: `spec/pdp64/pdp64.hbnf` (header, `encoding-name`, `regs`, `hot3`, `hot4`, `fused`, `%start insn`) includes `../generated/pdp64-forms.hbnf` first and `../pdp64-enc.hbnf`; `spec/pdp64-enc.hbnf` (the header that defines every name, `acc-8 .. acc-1`) includes the shapes and `mn-ext`. The leaf list was not split further: one generated file is the whole member grammar's generated part, because the forms, the leaves and the catalogue are one decision of the optimiser (errata 170: the leaf order is the prefix code) and the generator cannot change one without the others. The register, hot-table and fused rules are hand text (the writer read them back from the grammar, so they were never decided by it). **(3)** The generator writes only these files: `addforms.py --grammar-out spec/generated` (and `export.py --write-grammar`, `export_forms.py`, through `write_hbnf.py`); `keep_hand_text()`, the committed-text preservation and the member-name and header strings of the first generation in the writer are deleted, unnecessary now. **(4)** `%start insn` (hbnf: "the root is the first rule the file you run defines, `%start` pins it") and the include of the forms first keep `insn` the first rule, so hbnf's C for the grammar is byte for byte what it was. The rules of the hand-edited tail (name, registers, hot tables, fused) now follow the generated ones, and `mn-ext` stands before the `acc-N` rules: the only changes of order, none of them meaning anything to a tool. **(5)** `tools/encgram.py` follows `include` as hbnf does (relative to the including file, a file read once, `%start` skipped); `tools/vec-glance.py` reads the family words from `generated/pdp64-forms.hbnf`; every other reader goes through `encgram`. **(6)** `tools/check-grammar-enc.sh`: the byte-for-byte check of the committed grammars is now the same strength on each generated file (the three files written again into a scratch directory must equal `spec/generated/`; a stale file fails), and a second check requires the `GENERATED` first line, that each generated file is included by a master, that no master defines a rule a generated file defines, and that no hand-edited file carries the line. *Proof:* the fully resolved rule set read with `encgram.read_rules()` before and after has the same 750 rules with the same normalised bodies (a sorted diff is empty); `encgram.load()` returns the same dictionary (JSON, sorted keys); hbnf's C for `spec/pdp64/pdp64.hbnf` is byte for byte identical; `tests/enc/generated.sha256`, the codec tables, `tests/enc/vectors.txt`, the QEMU and LLVM generated files and the manual figures are unchanged (`git status` shows only the structural files, the generators, the checks and the documents); the workflow of errata 170 (`addforms.py`, `mkenc.py --md`, `check-grammar-enc.sh --update`, `mk_vectors.py`) run on the tree wrote no file (`rsync -rcn` against a snapshot). *Not done:* the copies in `build/` (`build/pdp64-mnemonics.hbnf` is the hand file plus the `mnemonic` rule of `tools/mkgrammar.py`; `build/x/pdp64-mnemonics.hbnf` adds `mn-ext` and the registers r16-r31) are untracked intermediates whose bytes `tests/enc/generated.sha256` records; they are left as they were.
180. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-18; the details the assistant's, R493): the grammar is a drop-in directory, `spec/grammars.d/`, of numbered parts; a new part is a new file and nothing else changes.** *The owner's words:* "on the grammars.... for this time, we can break the byte identical requirement. shit just has to work. just get it done in a way where new grammars can be dropped in for individual parts, perhaps something like the dropdir formalization, grammars.d/NN-gram1.hbnf etc."; his standing rule "any generated hbnf should be an include into the master hbnf file that is hand edited"; his refinement "and could even be grammars.d/NN-name/{prologue,grammar,epilogue}.hbnf so they can be assembled in their proper order."; and the future direction "later on a proper hbnf grammar would parse each hbnf file directly then do the shuffle so that prologues, grammars, and epilogues all get merged properly in the right sections of the 'one big grammar'". *The assistant's decisions:* **(1)** A part is `NN-name.hbnf` or a directory `NN-name/` of `prologue.hbnf`, `grammar.hbnf`, `epilogue.hbnf`; the grammar is every prologue, then every grammar, then every epilogue, each pass in `NN` order; a rule is defined once (a second `=` is an error naming both files; the one extension is hbnf's `=/`, which `sysreg =/ "hvctl"@13` and `forms =/ "CRY1"` use); each section says `; targets: asm enc spec` (which hbnf grammar reads it; `spec` is the numbers the generators read). **(2)** `spec/pdp64-asm.hbnf` and `spec/pdp64/pdp64.hbnf` are hand-edited masters of directives that include a GENERATED index (`spec/generated/index-asm.hbnf`, `index-enc.hbnf`, written by `tools/grammars_d.py`, the thin text-level stopgap; hbnf has `include` and no glob). **(3)** The old files were split by part: `00-ascii 01-common 05-registers 22-enc-vocabulary 23-enc-registers 30-mn-core 35-mn-numbers 40-mn-system 41-sysreg 42-conditions 50-system-v1 60-asm-syntax`; the generated parts are `20-enc-forms 21-enc-shapes 80-mn-ext` (`tools/encopt/addforms.py`), `10-enc-insn/prologue.hbnf` (`insn`, so it is the first rule) and `90-mnemonic/epilogue.hbnf` (`mnemonic`, after every group), both by `tools/mkparts.py`, which collects every `leaf-` and every `mn-` group of every part. **(4)** There are no copies of the grammars in `build/` any more (that was the wart of errata 179): hbnf reads the masters as they are; `r16`-`r31` are in `rnum` and `mn-ext` is a part. **(5)** Every tool reads the whole directory (`tools/grammars_d.py`; `encgram.load()`, `mkgrammar.enums()`); `tools/check-grammar-enc.sh` fails on a stale index, an unnumbered part, a repeated number, a duplicate rule, a part without targets, a directive outside a prologue, the GENERATED line misplaced. **(6)** Demonstrated: a family dropped in as two files (`98-example-words`, `99-example-enc`, shipped as `.disabled`) is assembled, in the codec tables, the manual and the test generators with no other edit. *Changed outputs:* the encoding C of hbnf, `build/pdp64-tab.c`, the accessors, the groups header and every golden file are byte-identical; `pdp64-consts.h` and `pdp64-names.h` gain `RNUM_R16..R31` (`rnum` is the 32 registers) and a new header line (the sibling `pcc/arch/pdp64/pdp64-names.h` was re-exported); `tests/enc/generated.sha256` lost the two copies of the grammar; the manual's prose names the new files; `tests/asmsyntax.c` says r16-r31 are registers (r32 is not). *Future:* hbnf itself to read the directory and merge the three passes (the owner's words above); the contract is in `spec/README.md` so that a native loader replaces the scripts with no part changing. Left: the encoding optimiser does not see a hand-written family's forms; the prefix of a hand-written leaf is chosen by hand (`mkenc.py` checks it).
181. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-19; the details the assistant's, R494 to R504): virtualisation is part of the design, and the eleven questions of the virtualisation form are answered.** This reverses the proposal of B.2.11.1 (virtualisation out of scope for System v1). *The owner's choices:* (1) every sensitive instruction traps, after an audit (R494); (2) a third, hypervisor mode with trap-control and exit-reason registers (R495); (3) two-stage translation in hardware (R496); (4) hardware virtual interrupt lines and a virtual timer offset (R497); (5) assigned devices through the IOMMU with a guest-visible second level (R498); (6) no nested virtualisation in version 1 (R499); (7) hidden vector state: rely on restart-from-entry and zeroing, nothing hidden observable (R500); (8) a full table of the state after a guest reset (R501); (9) a small in-house hypervisor on the QEMU target first (R502); (10) the manual: MMU in 6, IOMMU in 2.11, a short driver summary in 5.10, a new short chapter for virtualisation, and DMA placed close to the IOMMU (R503); (11) the IOMMU design is finished now, as proposals for review, then in the QEMU model (R504). *Where the owner went against the assistant's recommendation:* questions 2, 3, 4, 5 and 7 (the assistant had recommended the staged or software forms and clearing the hidden state). The owner's choice governs. Questions 2 to 5 are new hardware (a mode, a second table root, a guest tag, virtual interrupt lines, a timer offset, an IOMMU second level), which the assistant had counted as costs; the sizes are to be measured with the method of `docs/encoding-work/vec-gates.md` once the designs exist. *Question 7 is not closed:* errata 175 found that a handler naming accumulators with `vclr` outside a block leaves the naming for the next block; choosing to rely on the restart rule does not close that, so a proof or a closing rule is owed, and the manual may not claim that nothing hidden is observable across a world switch until it exists. *Not done, stated plainly:* no design of the hypervisor mode, the second stage, the virtual interrupt lines or the IOMMU exists; B.2.11 and J.2.3 now record the decisions and the open details; the new chapter on virtualisation, the move of the DMA text and the audit of sensitive instructions are not done; nothing is in the grammar, `as64`, `sim64`, QEMU or LLVM. *Where:* [`manual/appendix-h-proposed.md`](manual/appendix-h-proposed.md) B.2.11, [`manual/appendix-j-open.md`](manual/appendix-j-open.md) J.2.3.
182. **DONE (the owner's request, 2026-11-19; the details the assistant's, R505): the explanation of virtualization is in the kernel chapter, section 6.14.** The owner's words: "all this stuff you just said about what virtualization is used for, that fits the \"writing for first year student and old pdp11 asm programmer\" so include it in that appropriate chapter." *Done:* section 6.14 of `docs/manual-src/06-kernel.md` (the PDP-11 reader's starting point, trap and emulate, who needs virtualization, what lacking it means, what it costs, and the ten statements 6.14.1 to 6.14.10 of the decisions of errata 181); the old 6.14 (what was checked) is now 6.15. *The placement:* errata 181 (R503) decided "a new short chapter"; the assistant put the text in chapter 6 because a new chapter before the conclusion renumbers chapters 8 to 10 and every literal citation of them, and because the hypervisor mode and the second stage extend 6.2 and 6.6 to 6.8; it can be moved into a chapter of its own if the owner wants that. *A mistake, corrected the same day:* the owner also asked, for the owner's own understanding, to what degree nested virtualization is needed. The assistant wrote the case for and against nesting into 6.14, and its estimate of the number of new mnemonics into B.2.11.3, J.2.3 and errata 181, which the owner had not asked for; all of it was removed. The answers belong to the conversation, not to the manual; 6.14.7 states only the owner's decision (R499). *Not done, stated plainly:* the cost figures in 6.14 and the claims about other machines are general knowledge and say so; nothing about the pdp64's gates is measured. *Where:* [`manual/06-kernel.md`](manual/06-kernel.md) section 6.14.
183. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-19; the details the assistant's, R506): the facts that a grammar cannot hold are kept in one place, in git-controlled text files keyed by the base mnemonic.** The owner's words: "maybe we should have a db with a single source of truth for all opcodes/operands, for each one it will contain all facts about it, which will be used to generate various grammars, etc."; "grammar is main source of truth, but the db would hold facts that don't really apply in a grammar. git controlled text files are fine."; "the grammar could also hold the index into the db for that rule once matched"; "agree, the mnemonic is the key". *Done (step 1, the vec family):* `spec/facts/vec.toml` (65 tables: wave, meaning, the `llvm` flag), `tools/facts.py` (the loader; `--check` fails if the grammar has a base with no table or a table has no base, tested both ways), `tools/vec-glance.py` and `tools/gen-llvm-td.py` read it instead of their hand lists (`META` and a regular expression of 65 names); their outputs are byte-identical to the committed ones; the check is in `tools/check-all.sh`; the mechanism is in `spec/README.md`. The grammar keeps the names, encodings and shapes. *Not done, stated plainly:* the other places that keep a fact by hand are not converted: the manual's entries (`tools/manual/isa_vec.py`), the executor's name table (`common/pdp64exec_vec.inc`), the assembler's lane-type rules (`as64/as64.c`, which are code and need a table-driven rewrite), the benchmark groups (`llvm/bench/usage.py`), the gate tools and the test generators; the compiler's selection code (`PDP64VecLoop.cpp`) and the executor's semantics stay hand-written and can only be checked; the core instruction groups have no facts file yet; an explicit key in a grammar rule (`; fact: NAME`) is not needed yet. *Where:* `spec/facts/`, `tools/facts.py`, `spec/README.md` ("The facts about the words").
184. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-19; the details the assistant's, R507): "do it all": the hand-kept lists of facts about the words are converted to read `spec/facts/` where that is data, and checked where it is code.** The owner's words, in answer to the question of which of the remaining consumers to do next after step 1 of errata 183: "do it all". *Converted, each proven by identical output and one commit each:* **(1)** the manual's vec entries (`tools/manual/isa_vec.py`): the 29 entries are fields of `spec/facts/vec.toml` (`entry` on every base, and `entry_seq`, `title`, `syntax`, `op`, `notes`, `exc`, `cf` on the first base of each entry); the list of the 29 entries as Python dictionaries is identical before and after, and `mkmanual.py` writes the same manual and `--check` passes (the entries are all held (P), so no page changes). **(2)** the executor's name table of the vec family (`vbases[]` of `common/pdp64exec_vec.inc`): generated by `tools/gen-vec-names.py` from the field `exec` into `common/pdp64exec_vecbases.inc` (the same 65 pairs), copied to the QEMU target by `qemu/sync.sh` (the list there gained the file); the enum and the semantics stay hand-written, and the check says every `exec` is in the enum and has a `case`, and every `case VO_x` is the `exec` of a base (tested by mutation). **(3)** the assembler's lane-type check of the vec family (`vec_typecheck` of `as64/as64.c`): the per-word lists (the 30-base chain of the lane operations, the dot, compare, load and store lists, the three words that a 2D block refuses and the one whose floating-point sum fixes the order) are the class of each base, `asm`, `asm_no2d`, `asm_fsum` in the facts, generated into `as64/asmvecclass.inc`; the rule of each class stays code; `tests/asm`, `tests/asm-err` and all 237 assembler sources of the tree give the same bytes and messages with the old and the new `as64` (compared one by one), and `check-as64.sh` is green. **(4)** `llvm/bench/usage.py`: the groups (`group`), the reasons no kernel uses a base (`usage_why`) and the set the pass supports (`usage_supported`) are facts; its output is identical to the committed `llvm/vector-usage.md`. **(5)** the core instructions, scoped and converted where clean: `spec/facts/core.toml` has a table for each of the 286 core bases of the grammar (`exec`, `arg`, `exec_dotted`, `exec_cc`), and the executor's name table of the core (`hdefs[]` of `common/pdp64exec_enc.inc`, 257 rows) is generated from it by `tools/gen-core-names.py` into `common/pdp64exec_corehdefs.inc` (the same 257 rows, in another order: the order does not matter, the table is looked up by name; `check-sim64`, `check-exec-enc`, `check-timing`, `check-qemu` and the machine tests pass, QEMU rebuilt), with the same two checks (each `exec` has a `case`, each `case` is an `exec`) and the check of the keys against the grammar. *Checks added to `tools/facts.py --check` (new, not weakening any):* each table has its fields; each base names a manual entry that a base defines; each vec base has an executor handler (item 2); **each vec base is run by a test of `tests/sim` or `tests/qemu`** (a test that only assembles it does not count); where a table says the compiler selects the base (`compiler`, 53 bases, from `llvm/vector-usage.md`), the named file of `llvm/target/PDP64/` spells it, and a base the usage table says is emitted has the field; every word that a row of `tools/gates/silicon-total.py` or a class of `tools/gates/lat-table.py` names is a base (or one of the listed ordinary or removed words: `vector`, `vmemlat`, and the removed gather `vldx`, errata 170, whose row is the record of what was measured). *What the checks found:* no difference between a hand list and the grammar in any conversion (every converted table agrees with what the code did); **25 of the 65 vec bases had no test that executes them** (`vsub vmul vand vor vxor vshl vshr vsar vabd vabdu vmin vmax vminu vmaxu vneg vsubs vaddsu vsubsu vfnma vdotu vdotsu vstt vlseg4 vsseg3 vsseg4`: the simulator was never run on them; they were assembled by the encoding tests only), fixed: `tests/sim/vec-lane.s` (generated by `tests/sim/gen_vec_lane.py`, 186 tests against an independent model in Python, 77 elements, run also at the strips 16, 32 and 64) executes every one of them, with the register and immediate forms, the corners (sign boundary, all ones, a shift count at and above the lane width) and the segment and strided words; all 186 agree with the executor (no executor bug was found), and a model error in the first draft of the test of `vlseg4` (a pointer register that was also the end of the block) was caught by the test and fixed in the generator. 12 vec bases are not selected by the compiler (`vec2 vskip vfin vlseg3 vabdu vfmab vdot vdotu vdotsu vtbl vfir vclr`; the reasons are `usage_why` in the facts): a gap of the compiler, recorded, not a gap of the facts. *Check only, and why:* the gate tables (`tools/gates/silicon-total.py`, `lat-table.py`): a row is a measured block shared by several words, with its own synthesis, not a property of a word, so it is checked (the words it names exist) and not generated; the test generators (`tests/sim/gen_vec_*.py`): each carries its own independent model per word, so the word lists are checked by the test-coverage check; the compiler's selection code (`PDP64VecLoop.cpp`) and the executor's semantics are code. `tools/arch-compare/vsurvey-class.py` has no pdp64 class list (its classes are the regular expressions of the other instruction sets), so there was nothing to convert. *Left, with the reason:* **the manual's entries of the core instructions** (`tools/manual/isa_part1.py` to `isa_part4.py`, 127 entries; the section placement `HOME` of `tools/mkmanual.py`; the stories `isa_stories*.py`): they are keyed by the id of an entry, not by a mnemonic (an entry covers several words, and one word is in several entries: `movq` is in `MOV`, `MOVD`, `MOVUSER`, `MOVUSERD`), so a table for each base would repeat or split the entries; to convert them there must be a table of entries beside the tables of bases, which departs from the rule that the mnemonic is the key: the owner's to decide. The other core lists: the flag classes of `tools/flagreliance.py` (`WRITERS`, `READERS`, `MOVES`, `NEUTRAL`: regular expressions over assembly text), the layout derivations of `tools/gen-llvm-td.py` (the lists of words whose widest field it measures: not facts about a word), the core words of `tools/ooo-readiness.py` and `tools/archcmp.py` (classifications of a study), the per-word code of `as64` for the core (the `cbCC` expansion and the check sites): none converted, none checked. *Where:* `spec/facts/vec.toml`, `spec/facts/core.toml`, `tools/facts.py`, `tools/gen-vec-names.py`, `tools/gen-core-names.py`, `as64/asmvecclass.inc`, `common/pdp64exec_vecbases.inc`, `common/pdp64exec_corehdefs.inc`, `tests/sim/gen_vec_lane.py`, `spec/README.md` ("The facts about the words").
185. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-19; R508): `MOVQL` stays five parcels.** The owner's words: "i accept movql at 5 parcels". *Context:* the manual (9.1.4, the glossary, the table of 9.2) says a leaf is one, two or three parcels and that `MOVQL`, the one leaf with a 64-bit literal, is five parcels (ten bytes); the owner's question of 2026-11-04 (a 16, 32, 48 or 64-bit immediate coming right after the opcode and not counted in the parcels) was run through the allocation model and recorded as not adopted (R326). *Effect:* no text changes; 9.1.4 stands. *Measured the same day from `spec/pdp64/tables.md`:* of the 65,536 first-parcel patterns, 35,076 belong to 32 one-parcel leaves, 14,594 to 138 two-parcel leaves, 14,908 to 69 three-parcel leaves and 32 to `MOVQL`; 926 are unassigned (the count of errata 159, one illegal pattern, predates the vec family and the 2D block).
186. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-19; R509): the 14 decisions of the audit of sensitive instructions are accepted as recommended.** The owner answered the form of `docs/encoding-work/sensitive-instructions-audit.md` section 7: "Accept my recommendation" for each of the 14, with no comments. *The decisions, as the audit states them:* (1) a physical interrupt always exits to the hypervisor, whatever the guest's `PRI`; the guest's `PRI`, `SPL` and `MTPS` mask only virtual interrupts, by hardware (no trap on `SPL`); (2) `MFCPUID` becomes privileged, and each guest gets a hypervisor-set virtual core number that `MFCPUID` and `MFSR CPUID` return when a guest runs; (3) the hypervisor mode is kept outside the PSW (a separate state bit) and not in the current-mode field; the reserved mode value 2 stays reserved; (4) `HALT` always exits; no trap-control bit can turn it off; (5) a range of `TRAP #n` numbers is routed to the hypervisor by a trap-control mask as the hypercall (no new opcode); (6) `SEV` is scoped to the cores of the guest by the hypervisor; `WFE` traps by default and may be untrapped for a guest that owns a core; 7.10 states that `WFE` may return without an event and software must re-test; (7) `ICINV` and `ICINVA` stay unprivileged and untrapped; a bit that traps `ICINVA` only if timing between guests is a concern; the `ICINV`-needs-`ISYNC`-but-`ISYNC`-is-privileged oddity is to be resolved; (8) `MTDMW` and `DMW0/1` trap (the hypervisor emulates) when a window takes effect; (9) the vector fetch and the page-table walk of a guest go through stage 2 (a guest-physical `VBR` and `PTBR`); a stage-2 miss on either is an exit, not a machine check; (10) the hypervisor has its own exception registers (`HEPC`, `HEPSW`, `HEVA`, `HCAUSE` and a guest-physical fault address), because `EPC`, `EPSW`, `EVA` and `ECAUSE` are the guest's while a guest runs; (11) `PROBER` and `PROBEW` in a guest test both stages; a stage-2 miss exits so the probe re-runs after the hypervisor fills it; (12) (a) decide before the hypervisor design whether `UA`, `STUA` and `CLUA` exist (the manual and the grammar disagree); (b) `MFCPUID` is listed in 6.2.5 or 6.2.6 once decision 2 is made; (c) in a guest, `MFSR` and `MTSR` of a hypervisor-control register exit (6.14.7) and of an unassigned number raise illegal-instruction in the guest; (13) a world switch (and every exception entry) clears the naming of the vector accumulators and any `LL` reservation; this is architectural and mechanical, and closes the hole of errata 175 (it replaces the reliance of 6.14.9); (14) 6.14.2 states that no unprivileged instruction reads a clock, a counter or the core identity; if one is ever added it must have a trap-control bit. *Supersedes:* decision 13 replaces the choice of errata 181 (R500, 6.14.9) to rely on restart-from-entry and zeroing; the hole of errata 175 is closed by the clear at a world switch and at exception entry. *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is in the manual, the grammar, `as64`, `sim64` or QEMU yet; the manual text, the hypervisor registers and the exit-reason codes are to be shown to the owner before they are written; the audit's open facts (a multi-core `MFCPUID` was not run; the atomics, `LL`/`SC`, crypto and `MVAL` are not in the executor and could not be audited; whether the manual promises a re-test after `WFE` in every use) stay open.
187. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-19; R510): eleven of the 13 decisions of the IOMMU proposals are accepted as recommended; two are not decided.** The owner answered the form of `docs/encoding-work/iommu-proposals.md` section 9: "Accept my recommendation" for 2 to 9 and 11 to 13, "Not decided yet" for 1 and 10, with no comments. *Accepted, as the proposals state them:* (2) the device table: a 16-bit identity, one identity space, a directory of 512 entries of 8 bytes and a leaf page of 128 entries of 32 bytes (a 9 and 7 bit split), a mode field with blocked, one level and two levels, blocked at reset; (3) the device entry: 32 bytes with V, MUTE, STAGES (A, B, A then B), levels per stage, domain and guest tags, two root words and a reserved word kept for the interrupt table; reserved bits must be zero or the device is refused; no bypass mode; (4) the leaf: V, R, W and the page number; W needs R; X, U, G and MT must be zero; A and D ignored; large pages allowed; 3, 4 or 5 levels per device; bits 40 to 63 left unallocated; (5) one unit at the root complex, its registers in the root complex's block (J.2.5), not a PCI Express function; devices that a third-party switch lets talk directly form a group assigned together, the one exception to "each device its own domain"; (6) a read is answered with an error completion; a posted write is dropped and counted; every refusal is logged in a ring in memory with an interrupt, a sticky overflow bit and count and a per-device mute bit; fail closed on a bad table; never all ones, never a machine check; the hypervisor forwards level-A faults to the guest's virtual log; (7) cached translations keyed by (requester identity, guest tag, domain tag, page, level); commands invalidate device, domain, guest, all and sync; hardware discards everything on a change of the device-table pointer, on the reset bit and on reset; invalidate device does not complete until in-flight requests are finished or refused; the guard that blocks a device whose guest is being invalidated; (8) the guest tag is the same number space and width as the processor's (0 is the host); the guest reset sequence of section 5 of the proposals is the hypervisor's duty; (9) in version 1 the guest's unit is virtual and trapped, the hypervisor forwards commands and faults; no guest-visible hardware ring; (11) the register block: 64-bit registers at 8-byte spacing; the first eight as the command ring (ID, CONTROL, STATUS, RING_BASE, RING_SIZE, DOORBELL, IRQ, DEVICE = the device-table pointer), then CAPS, the fault ring registers and the invalidate register; both a command ring and an invalidate register on one executor; (12) the unit's own interrupt is a wired line to the CPU like the timer's in the model, to be replaced when J.6.3 is decided; word 3 of the device entry reserved for an interrupt-remapping pointer; (13) the model: a PCI Express host bridge and our own DMA test function added to `pdp64-virt` first; a model cache so that a missing invalidate is observable; the tests of section 8 of the proposals including the scratch-edit non-vacuity check; the nested test plays both roles because the model has no hypervisor mode. *Not decided:* (1) what "a second level that the guest can see" (6.14.6, errata 181) means: the proposals read it as the guest writing level A, a table translating guest-virtual to guest-physical addresses for its device, before the hypervisor's level B; decisions 3, 7, 8 and 9 are accepted as recommended but assume that reading (two stages, a guest tag, a virtual unit for the guest), so they stand only if the reading is confirmed; (10) whether the unit offers ATS and page requests (the proposals recommended not in version 1, the unit answering a translation request with an error). *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is in the manual, the grammar, `as64`, `sim64` or QEMU; the proposals say that constants for the unit would need a grammar addition and the owner's approval, which this answer does not give by itself; the walk-count discrepancy with 6.14 of the proposals' decision 14 was corrected in 6.14 the same day (15 memory references against 3).
188. **CORRECTION (the assistant's, 2026-11-19; R511; no new decision; made on the owner's "yes, fix them and push"): the hole of the vector accumulator naming is in errata 172, and audit decision 13 adds to the restart from entry, it does not replace it.** Errata 181 and 186, the manual (6.14.9, B.2.11.4), the audit document and the ledger cited errata 175 for a hole in which a handler that names accumulators with `vclr` outside a block leaves the naming set for the interrupted program's next block; that is the item "Not fixed, open" of errata 172 (errata 175 is the fixed strip of 16 bytes for a block with accumulators and the read-out by `vfin`). Errata 186 and R509 said that decision 13 (a world switch and every exception entry clear the naming of the vector accumulators and any `LL` reservation) replaces the choice of R500 to rely on restart-from-entry. It does not: R500 stands, the block restarts from its entry with the hardware's hidden copy of the naming made at the `vec`, and a strip starts with its registers zero; decision 13 adds the closing rule that errata 172 said was missing ("it needs the hardware to clear the naming at `rti`"), so the proof that was owed under R500 is replaced by a rule. *Corrected in place:* manual 6.14.9 and B.2.11.4 (the citation), the ledger, the audit document. *Not changed:* the text of errata 181 and 186 and rows R500 and R509, which are pushed history; read them with this erratum. *Not done:* the manual text of 6.14.9 still states R500 alone; it is to be rewritten with the other audit decisions after the owner has seen it.
189. **DONE (the owner's request, 2026-11-19; R512): the manual text of the audit decisions (errata 186) and the IOMMU decisions (errata 187) is written.** The owner's words: "write the manual text from the draft". *Written:* **chapter 6:** `MFCPUID` in the privileged list 6.2.5; 6.14.2 to 6.14.6 and 6.14.9 rewritten and 6.14.11 to 6.14.14 added (the audit's decisions 1 to 11 and 13, 14: a physical interrupt always exits and a guest's priority masks only virtual interrupts; the hypervisor mode outside the status word, with its own exception registers; the vector fetch, the page-table walk and `PROBER`/`PROBEW` through the second stage; `MFCPUID` privileged with a virtual core number; `HALT` always exits, the hypercall as routed `TRAP` numbers, `WFE` trapped by default and `SEV` scoped; `ICINV` and `ICINVA` untrapped; `MTDMW` trapped; the clear of the accumulator naming and `LL` reservation at a world switch and at exception entry, replacing the earlier wording of 6.14.9 as errata 188 explains; no unprivileged clock); **chapter 7:** one sentence in 7.10.1 (`WFE` may return with no event); **chapter 2:** 2.11.1 to 2.11.6 (the device table, the leaf, one unit at the root complex, the reports of a refusal, the registers, the unit's interrupt); **chapter 5:** a sentence in the Addresses bullet of 5.10 that points to 2.11 (the text of 2.11 and 5.10 point to each other; the sections are not moved, which the owner has not asked for); **appendix J:** J.2.3 shortened to what is open (the meaning of a second level that the guest can see, and translation requests), with the four accepted statements that depend on the first question held there; two new open items, J.6.13 (do `UA`, `STUA` and `CLUA` exist: audit decision 12a) and J.6.14 (`ICINV` needs `ISYNC`, which is privileged: audit decision 7), with options and the assistant's leanings, for the owner to decide; **appendix H:** B.2.11 is one line that points to 6.14. *Not written, and why:* the four statements of the draft that depend on the undecided first question (the entry of a device, the cached translations, the guest tag, the virtual unit) are in J.2.3, not in 2.11, because the body holds only decided material; the registers, the exit codes, the entry and exit sequence and the instruction encodings, which are designs. *Not changed:* the sections of chapters 2 and 5 are not moved. *Checks:* `python3 tools/mkmanual.py --check`, `tools/check-manual-src.py` and `tools/mkbook.sh --check` (buildabook: 29 chapter files, numbers agree, all tokens resolved); the book was rebuilt. *Where:* `docs/manual-src/` (06-kernel, 07-smp, 02-bus, 05-drivers, appendix-parts 02-open, 06-open, b-proposed).
190. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-19, and the assistant's on the owner's instruction; R513): IOMMU decisions 1 and 10 are answered, and the two J.6 questions of errata 189 are decided.** The owner's words: "accept 1 and 3, decide the J.6 items". *Read as:* options 1 and 3 of J.2.3, the options that the assistant had named as its leaning: (1) **the guest writes a level of its own (level A)**, the hypervisor's being level B, the unit's real table staying the host's and the guest seeing a virtual unit; (3) **translation requests (ATS and page requests) are not offered in version 1**, the unit answering a translation request with an error.
