These close IOMMU decisions 1 and 10 of errata 187, so the four statements that were held in J.2.3 are now decided and are statements of 2.11 (2.11.2, 2.11.3, 2.11.7, 2.11.8, with 2.11.9 for the translation requests; 2.11 now has 2.11.1 to 2.11.11), and J.2.3 is deleted from appendix J: J.2.4 and J.2.5 are renumbered J.2.3 and J.2.4 and their citations follow (the manual, `docs/pdp64-isa.md`, `docs/pdp64-q64-bus.md`, `docs/pdp64-fpga-plan.md`, the ledger, `tools/feedback/mkfeedback.py`; the form `docs/feedback/decisions-feedback.txt` was regenerated; the errata, the register and the two review documents keep the old numbers, which were those of their day). *The two J.6 decisions, which the owner delegated (the assistant decided; the owner may overrule):* **J.6.13: `UA`, `STUA` and `CLUA` exist**, as the decided statements 6.10.1 and 6.13.7 say; the manual is unchanged there and the grammar and the executor are to follow. *Basis, checked in the QEMU sources (v11.1.2):* x86 has `CR4.SMAP` (`target/i386/cpu.h`, used in `target/i386/tcg/system/excp_helper.c`), ARM has `PSTATE.PAN` (`target/arm/cpu.h`, used in `target/arm/ptw.c`), RISC-V has `SUM` (`target/riscv/cpu_bits.h`, used in `target/riscv/tcg/cpu_helper.c`): in each, a bit of the status state decides whether supervisor data accesses reach user pages. **J.6.14: `ISYNC` is not privileged**, so that the sequence of 7.12.3 (write the instructions, `ICINV`, `ISYNC`) works for a program that generates code, as `ICINV` and `ICINVA` already are not privileged. *Basis, checked in the QEMU sources:* ARM's `ISB` is translated with no privilege check (`target/arm/tcg/translate-a64.c`, `trans_ISB`) and RISC-V's `FENCE.I` is translated with no privilege check (`target/riscv/tcg/insn_trans/trans_rvi.c.inc`, `trans_fence_i`, only the `Zifencei` extension is required): both are the unprivileged synchronisation that follows an instruction-cache invalidation. The writes to `PTBR`, `ASID`, `MMUCTL` and `VBR` that `ISYNC` also covers are themselves privileged, so an unprivileged `ISYNC` gives a program no extra power. *Changed in the manual:* 6.2.5 (`ISYNC` leaves the privileged list), 6.2.6 (`ISYNC` is listed as not privileged, with the reason), the entry of `ISYNC` in 9.14, and 6.8.5 (its pointers to B.2.11 and J.2.3 now name 6.14.8 and 2.11.7); J.6.13 and J.6.14 are deleted (they were new and unreferenced). *Not done, stated plainly:* the grammar comment on `isync` (`spec/grammars.d/40-mn-system.hbnf`) and the executor (`common/pdp64exec.c`, `priv()` before `isync`) still make `ISYNC` privileged, and the executor flushes the whole translation buffer on it, which is allowed (an implementation may discard more) but is a cost for an unprivileged instruction; `UA`, `STUA` and `CLUA` are not in the grammar or the executor; the tests; each is listed in `docs/implementation-status.md`. The introduction of 6.14 is being rewritten with checked facts only (the owner's "none of this unverified general knowledge, be accurate, put in the work, verify!"); it will be recorded when done.
191. **DONE (the owner's instruction, 2026-11-19; R514): the introduction of 6.14 is rewritten with checked facts only, and the statements in it that were wrong or unsupported are corrected.** The owner's words: "6.14 none of this unverified general knowledge, be accurate, put in the work, verify!". *How it was checked:* a research agent read the primary sources and quoted them; the assistant then looked for each quoted sentence in the saved documents by text search (the *PDP-11 Processor Handbook* 1979, Popek and Goldberg 1974, Creasy 1981, Adams and Agesen 2006, Bhargava et al. 2008), read the abstract of Hall and Robinson 1991 on the page image, and fetched the vendor and project pages itself (AWS, Microsoft, Docker, the Android Virtualization Framework, QNX, GitHub, the Linux `arch/x86/kvm/Kconfig`, OpenBSD `vmm(4)`); every sentence now quoted in 6.14 was found. *What was wrong or unsupported in the earlier text, and what the section says now:* (1) "The idea is older than the PDP-11: IBM's VM/370 (1972)" was wrong: VM/370 (1972) is later than the PDP-11; CP and CMS were in production use from 1967 and VM/370 brought them to the System/370 in 1972 (Creasy). (2) The example "a guest kernel executes `MTPS` or writes a page register, the machine takes a trap" had no source; it is replaced by what the handbook says (`HALT` traps outside kernel mode; `RESET` and `SPL` are ignored there, with no trap). (3) The paraphrase of Popek and Goldberg ("every instruction that reads or changes the real state of the machine must trap") was imprecise: the section now quotes Theorem 1 and their definitions of privileged (traps in user mode and not in supervisor mode), control sensitive and behavior sensitive, with their own example, `JRST 1` on the PDP-10. (4) "The cure [for the VAX] was a change to the instruction set" is replaced by the abstract of Hall and Robinson (modifications to the VAX architecture; several instructions sensitive but not privileged). (5) "The PDP-11 has such instructions *(unverified)*" is now verified (`SPL`, `RESET`). (6) The table of who uses virtualization is rewritten with one quoted sentence from the documentation of each product; "industrial controllers", the Android Emulator and the QNX safety certification are not stated because they were not confirmed (the Android Emulator sentence was not on the page that was fetched). (7) "Linux, OpenBSD and the cloud software built on them assume a hardware hypervisor mode" is replaced by the sentences of the KVM Kconfig and of `vmm(4)`; "and the cloud software built on them" is dropped. (8) The system-call cost is quoted from Adams and Agesen with its context (VMware's software monitor), not stated as general. (9) The guess of a few percent of the scalar core's gates is removed; what remains of the cost paragraph is what the design adds, the gate counts that exist (`docs/encoding-work/vec-gates.md`, a record of 2026-11-17), and the two-stage count nm + n + m from Bhargava et al. (15 for three levels at each stage against 3, counting table entries and not the data access: the earlier correction of errata 187 to "15 against 3" stands and is now sourced). (10) The sentence about machines that need no virtualization is dropped (a judgement, not a fact). (11) The bullet "Unverified in 6.14" of 6.15 is replaced by a bullet "Checked in 6.14" that lists the sources and what was not checked. *Not stated, because not verified:* the date of the PDP-11's announcement; industrial controllers; the Android Emulator's hypervisor; the safety certification of the QNX hypervisor. *Where:* `docs/manual-src/06-kernel.md` 6.14 and 6.15; the book was rebuilt (690 pages).

192. **DONE (the owner's instruction, 2026-11-19; R515): the decided changes of the virtualization work that need no new design are implemented: `ISYNC` unprivileged, `MFCPUID` privileged, `UA`, `STUA` and `CLUA`, and the clear of the vector accumulator naming at exception entry and return.** The owner's words: "do it all", in answer to the offer to start the implementation of the virtualization decisions (errata 186, 190). *1. `ISYNC` (errata 190):* the privilege check is removed from `common/pdp64exec.c`; the executor still flushes the whole translation buffer on a kernel-mode `ISYNC`, and a user-mode one flushes nothing (it has nothing to publish: the writes it covers are privileged); the comment on `isync` in `spec/grammars.d/40-mn-system.hbnf` changed and no generated file did (`tools/check-grammar-enc.sh` needed no `--update` for it). Tests: `tests/sim/isync-user.s` (user mode: no fault, `ICINV` then `ISYNC`, `ICINVA`, `HINT`; its `.exit` of 139 is gone), `isync-kernel.s`, an `isync` after `icinv` in the user block of `sys-user.S`; sim64 and QEMU. *2. `MFCPUID` (errata 186 decision 2):* `priv()` before it. The only program that executed it in user mode was `tests/sim/sys-user.S` (the block of unprivileged instructions); no other test, no program of `llvm/tests`, no runtime in this repository. The manual entries were wrong and are corrected: `MFCPUID` said "None" for privilege, `ICINV` said privileged (6.2.6 says it is not). Tests: `mfcpuid-user.s` (139), `mfcpuid-kernel.s`, `mfcpuid-priv.S` (sim64 -K: vector 3, EPC, the register unchanged). The virtual core number of a guest is part of the hypervisor design and is not done. *3. `UA`, `STUA`, `CLUA`:* **the encoding needed no new leaf and moved nothing**: the two words took the sub-opcodes of the reserved words `syspg0` and `syspg1` in the leaf `SYS0-1` (5-bit sub-opcode, 32 of 32 in use, two of them reserved), so they are one-parcel words, `f7de` and `f7df`; `syspg2` to `syspg5` stay reserved (four words: the entry `SYSPG` and J.9.6 say four). The edit is the rename of two words in the generated part `20-enc-forms.hbnf` (which `tools/encopt/addforms.py` writes back byte for byte, as it reads the committed grammar), the same in `docs/codegen-data/encoding/cat.py`, the hand group `mn-sysx` (`stua`@4, `clua`@5, the numbers of errata 43), the rule `psw` of `50-system-v1.hbnf` (`ua`@12), then `addforms.py`, `mkparts.py`, `grammars_d.py --write`, `mkenc.py --md`, `check-grammar-enc.sh --update` (the generated copies that changed: `pdp64-tab.c`, `pdp64-extnames.h`, `pdp64-mnnames.h`; the `80-mn-ext` numbering moved by two words, no encoding), the three test generators (no change), `tools/gen-core-names.py`, `tools/gen-llvm-td.py`, `tools/export-pcc-headers.sh`. Executor: the bit is in `PSW_VALID`; `STUA` and `CLUA` are privileged, set and clear it, take a non-zero pad as illegal; exception entry clears it and `EPSW` keeps the old value; `RTI` restores it; `MTPS` writes it from kernel mode and user mode cannot. QEMU (`qemu/target-pdp64`): a fourth MMU index, kernel with `UA` clear, so `STUA` and `CLUA` flush nothing; a kernel-mode load or store to a page whose `U` bit is set takes the load or store protection fault (vector 10 or 11, `ECAUSE` the vector, `EVA` the address, `EPC` the instruction, as the matrix of `mmu-matrix-kernel.S` shows for the rights); the instruction fetch of a user page is the same fault as before (`IPROT`) with `UA` set or clear; `PROBER` and `PROBEW` are not affected. **The `.U` moves are done in QEMU:** the executor sets `uacc` around the memory operand of a `.u` word (the facts table marks them, `H_USER`), the host uses the user MMU index for it, and an exception entered while it is set sets bit 15 of `ECAUSE` (6.10.4); sim64 has no MMU, so there a `.u` move is still a plain move. Tests: `tests/qemu/mmu-ua.S` (a kernel access to a user page faults with `UA` clear, works after `STUA`, faults again after `CLUA` with no flush, entry clears `UA` while `EPSW` keeps it and `RTI` restores it, fetch, `PROBER`, `PROBEW`, the `.U` moves with bit 15, a plain fault after a `.U` fault has none), `tests/sim/ua-psw.S` (-K), `tests/asm/sysua`, `tests/asm-err/stua-operand`, `clua-operand`, `tests/sim/unimpl-syspg2` (was syspg0); `mmu-matrix-kernel.S` and `mini-kernel.S` now execute `STUA` first (a kernel at reset has `UA` clear); `sys-psw.S` and `sysreg-system.S` now expect bit 12 (they expected it reserved). Each QEMU check was made to fail by removing `CLUA`, `STUA` or the `.U` of a move. *4. The naming of the vector accumulators (errata 186 decision 13, 188):* `C->vacc` is cleared at every exception entry (`enter()`, which faults, interrupts and traps use) **and at every exception return (`RTI`, `RTT`)**; the return is added to the decision because a handler that names accumulators with `VCLR` after the entry would otherwise leave them named for the interrupted program (the open item of errata 172, which says the hardware must clear at `RTI`); 6.14.9 now says entry and return. The hardware's hidden copy (`vrun_*`, `vrst_*`) is not touched, so a block restarts from its entry as before. `LL`: the atomics are not in the executor, so there is no reservation to clear; a world switch does not exist yet. Tests: `gen_vec_restart.py` has a handler variant, clob 4 (`VCLR` of all sixteen registers outside any block), run for each of the 56 kernels (912 scenarios, 856 before), `vec-restart.S` regenerated; `tests/sim/vec-clear.S` (the naming is cleared by the entry, by the return and for a block that the handler runs at once); both fail when either clear is removed; sim64 at the strips 16, 32 and 64 (`tools/strip-equiv.py` passes with the new tests); QEMU has no strip option and runs at the executor's 32. *Found:* (a) **the clear at entry makes a `VCLR` placed before the `VEC` unsafe against an interrupt taken between the two**: the naming is lost and the block then treats those registers as ordinary (`tests/sim/vec-clear.S` check 2 shows it); a block whose `VCLR` is inside it is not affected; the hand micro-kernels that name before the `VEC` (`k_sgemm_nt.ukr.s`) are exposed; this follows from decision 13 as worded and the owner may want the naming made before a `VEC` part of what an interrupt keeps, or those kernels moved into the block; not decided here. (b) The manual's `MFCPUID` and `ICINV` entries disagreed with 6.2.5 and 6.2.6 (corrected). (c) Two of the six reserved system words are used; the hypervisor design proposes a third (`GENTER`). *Not done:* the virtual core number of a guest; the world switch; the `LL` reservation; `sim64` has no MMU, so the guard has no effect there and the `.U` moves are plain; the `.U` form of the atomics; the entries of the `.U` words stay held back (P). *Evidence:* `tools/check-all.sh` exit 0 (sim64 147 run, 0 failed; QEMU 72 run, 0 failed, 4 skipped; relax 48 run, 2 failed, the known baseline); `llvm/check.sh` 61 `.ll`, 88 vector programs; `tools/strip-equiv.py` ok.
193. **CORRECTION (the assistant's, 2026-11-19; R516; no new decision): two statements of chapter 9 and one of chapter 1 were out of date, and are corrected.** Found by the review of the fit to seL4 (`docs/encoding-work/sel4-fit.md`) and checked by the assistant against the text and the generated tables. (1) **9.2.6** said "the machine has no hidden instruction state" *(D)*; errata 172, 173 and 186 and 6.14.9 decide that a vector block has hidden state (the naming of its accumulators and a hidden copy of the pointers) and restarts from its entry (the owner, errata 172: "hidden state is ok if we have precise exceptions and clean restarts"). 9.2.6 now says "no hidden instruction state, except the hidden state of a vector block, which restarts from the entry of the block (6.14.9, errata 172)", and the improvements paragraph of chapter 1 says the same in short. (2) **The counts** of 9.1.2 ("207 forms in 268 leaves"), 9.1.4 ("36, 166 and 65" leaves of one, two and three parcels) and 9.19.1 ("304 instruction words in 268 leaves") are those of an earlier grammar; the generated tables (`spec/pdp64/tables.md`) have 182 forms in 240 leaves, 32, 138 and 69 leaves of one, two and three parcels and one leaf (`MOVQL`) of five, and 685 mnemonics of which 365 are in the vector family and 320 are not. The sentences now say that. The counts are hand text in `docs/manual-src/09-isa.md`: they will go stale again when the grammar changes, and the rule of `spec/README.md` (every number is stated once and generated downstream) is not met for them; making them generated is not done. *Where:* `docs/manual-src/09-isa.md` 9.1.2, 9.1.4, 9.2.6, 9.19.1; `docs/manual-src/01-introduction.md`.

194. **DONE (the owner's instruction, 2026-11-19; R517): the IOMMU is built into the QEMU model, with a PCI Express host bridge and a DMA test function, the unit's numbers in one grammar part, and seven tests that were each checked against scratch mutations of the model.** The owner's words: "do it all", in answer to the offer to build the IOMMU into the QEMU model (decided: errata 181 question 11, errata 187 decision 13, errata 190). The specification is the decided text of 2.11.1 to 2.11.11 and, for what that text calls "to be determined", the recommendation of `docs/encoding-work/iommu-proposals.md` (sections 2 to 8); the points where the model took the proposal's recommendation or had to add something are listed under *Details*. *1. The host bridge (`qemu/hw-pdp64/virt.c`, kernel-mode machine only):* QEMU's generic `gpex` host, placed as the Arm and RISC-V virt machines place it: the configuration window (ECAM, one megabyte for each bus, buses 0 to 15; the bridge itself is bus 0, device 0), the memory window (the PCI memory address space is the system address space, so a base address register holds a physical address) and the I/O window; the four interrupt pins go to the new vectors `pcie-inta` to `pcie-intd` (35 to 38) at level `pcie` 5 (rule `vector`, rule `irq-level`); the unit's own line is vector `iommu` 39 at level 5. *The memory map is provisional* (the bit-39 device space of errata 97 is retired; J.2.1 is open); it is one place, the rule `map` of `spec/grammars.d/50-system-v1.hbnf`, and every address of the machine file and of the tests comes from there: IOMMU register block `0x8000002000` (the page after the console), ECAM `0x8100000000` (16 MiB), memory window `0x8200000000` (256 MiB), I/O window `0x8300000000` (64 KiB); the PCI Express windows lie above `dev-end` (`0x8010000000`, where nothing answers: `tests/sim/sys-exc.S`) so that nothing is moved or clashes. *2. The DMA test function (`qemu/hw-pdp64/dmatest.c`, `-device pdp64-dmatest`):* a PCI function of our own (vendor 0x1234, device 0x64d0), one 64-bit memory base address register of 8 KiB: registers for the source and destination device address, the length, start, a status with done and error, the result (the `MemTxResult` of a read), an interrupt enable (pin A), and a 4 KB buffer that the processor reads and writes; one transfer runs at once through `pci_dma_read` and `pci_dma_write`, so the IOMMU is in the path; a read that fails leaves the buffer alone and sets error and a nonzero result, a posted write has no answer and the function sees no error. *3. The unit (`qemu/hw-pdp64/iommu.c`, device `pdp64-iommu`, always in the kernel-mode machine, attached with `pci_setup_iommu`, one IOMMU memory region for each function, its translate method the unit):* the device table (a mode field, blocked at reset, one level or two levels; a directory of 512 entries of 8 bytes and leaf pages of 128 entries of 32 bytes), the device entry (V, MUTE, stages A, B and A then B, the levels of each, the domain and guest tags, two roots, the reserved word), the leaf entry (the processor's entry: V, R, W and the page number; W needs R; X, U, G, the memory type and bits 10, 11 and 40 to 63 must be zero; A and D ignored; 4 KB, 2 MB and 1 GB pages, aligned; 3, 4 or 5 levels), the walk of level A then level B with every level-A table pointer sent through level B, the error completion and the drop with a count, the fault ring in memory with the interrupt, the sticky overflow bit and count and the per-device mute bit, fail closed on a bad table (a table that is not in RAM, or cannot be read, is refused with the cause "table read" and the sticky table-error bit; never all ones, never a machine check), the command ring and the invalidate register on one executor (invalidate device, domain, guest and all, and sync), the unit's cache of device entries and translations keyed by (requester identity, guest tag, domain tag, page, level), the discard on a change of the device-table pointer, on the reset bit and on reset, and the wired interrupt line. *4. The numbers* are the new part `spec/grammars.d/51-iommu-v1.hbnf` (`; targets: spec`, numbers only, no instruction encoding changed): the registers, the bits, the device and directory entry, the command and fault record fields, the operation codes, the stages, the causes and the registers of the test function; generated to `pdp64-consts.h` (`IOMMU_*`, `DMATEST_*`) and the manual's constants page (`docs/manual/dictionary/constants.md`, a new section; the parser of `tools/mkmanual.py` now keeps the hyphenated names of the `spec` target, which it had dropped: the vector table of the constants page now lists `machine-check`, `irq-base`, `console-in`, `console-out` and `trap-base` too). No prose was added to the manual body. *5. The tests (`tests/qemu`, all on the kernel-mode machine; the routines they share are `iommu-lib.inc`, the macros `iommu-lib.h`):* `pcie-enum` (the host bridge: identifiers, class, empty positions read all ones); `pcie-dmatest` (the function: enumeration, sizing of the base address register, assignment, memory space and bus mastering, the buffer, DMA both ways, the interrupt); `iommu-enum` (a: the unit's registers: identification, capabilities, reset values, read-only registers, the registers that hold what is written, the mode that does not exist); `iommu-refuse` (b: no device entry, no mapping, a read-only mapping, another function's window not mapped, the interrupt taken only when the priority allows, a full fault ring with the overflow bit and count, a muted device, logging off); `iommu-map` (c: a write and a read through a 4 KB page and a 2 MB page, 3, 4 and 5 levels, the range cause, A and D clear, ten bad leaves, a pointer at level 0, V clear, a misaligned large page); `iommu-stale` (d: a stale translation is observable after a changed page-table entry and a changed device entry, and gone after invalidate device by the register and by the command ring, invalidate domain, guest and all, each with sync where the architecture waits; a command for another device, domain or guest leaves it; the change of the device-table pointer and the reset bit need no command; the one-level mode; a command that cannot be decoded; two guests with the same device addresses); `iommu-nested` (e: A then B: the composed translation, level-A tables at guest-physical addresses that are not the identity on the host, a fault in A reported with stage A, a fault in B with stage B and the guest-physical address, a read-only page in B, a table of A that B does not map, level B only, the guest reset sequence, two guests with the same level A and different level B; the test plays both roles); `iommu-blocked` (f and g: blocked at reset, the cause "blocked", the two-level mode works, the reset bit blocks again; ten malformed entries and three tables that cannot be read all refused with nothing delivered, the sticky bit and the interrupt, no machine check, no bus fault; a control with the tables repaired). `tools/check-qemu.sh`: 80 run, 0 failed, 4 skipped (73 before). *6. Non-vacuity (as errata 178 did for `mmu-flush`):* in a scratch copy of the model in the QEMU source tree (never in the repository; the genuine files were put back by `qemu/install.sh` and rebuilt), each of the following changes was selected at run time and the test that is named failed at the check that is named (the unmutated copy passed all seven): *iommu-enum:* the version of the ID register changed, a read-only count made writable, the sticky status bits made not clearable; *iommu-refuse:* a refused access passed through unchanged (bypass), a refused write not counted, the interrupt never raised, a refused read answered with all ones, no record written, the overflow bit missing from the record after a loss, the mute bit ignored, a write refused without regard to a read-only leaf, the lost-record count never kept, the result of a failed read always zero; *iommu-map:* the translation one page off, the bypass, the X bit, then U, G and the memory type, then bits 10, 11 and 40 to 63 not checked, W without R accepted, a large page not checked for alignment, the range check removed; *iommu-stale:* each command's discard removed in turn (invalidate device, domain, guest, all), invalidate device dropping only the entry and not the translations, no discard on a change of the device-table pointer, none on the reset bit, no cache at all (the stale check fails), the ring's doorbell not running the ring, sync writing nothing; *iommu-nested:* the level-A table pointers not sent through level B, a level-A fault reported as stage B, level B left out, the rights of level B ignored, no cache, invalidate guest not discarding; *iommu-blocked:* the bypass, a blocked unit passing addresses through, a bad table passing the access through, a reserved bit of the device entry not checked, a refused read answered with all ones, the sticky table-error bit not set; *pcie-dmatest:* the length ignored, the interrupt never raised; *pcie-enum:* the binary built before the host bridge (it times out at the first access to the window). *7. Details taken from the proposals or added by the model (the manual leaves them "to be determined"):* the registers are those of 2.11.10 with the proposals' offsets except that the placeholder `IRQ_ADDR` is left out (the unit's interrupt is a wired line, 2.11.11), so `CMD_HEAD` is at 0x48; two registers are added that the proposals do not list, `REFUSED_READS` (0x88) and `DROPPED_WRITES` (0x90), because "dropped and counted" (2.11.6) needs a count a program can read; `IRQ` reads the vector and is read only; the tag width is 16 bits (as the processor's address-space number; 6.14.4 leaves it open); the unit-type code and version are 0x1011 and 1; a command is 16 bytes and `sync` writes the value 1 to an 8-byte-aligned physical address named in word 1 (the proposals left the value to be settled); a write to `FAULT_DROPPED` of any value clears the count (the proposals left read-clears or write-clears to be settled); a write to the size or the base of a ring starts it over; the interrupt line is level-sensitive: asserted while the fault ring is not empty, or overflow or table error is set, and the fault-interrupt bit is on (the proposals say "on empty to non-empty"); a record has the cause in bits 0 to 7, the stage (device table 0, A 1, B 2) in bits 8 and 9, the write bit 10, a valid bit 62 and an overflow bit 63 (records were lost before this one); a fault at stage B carries the guest-physical address in word 3, and a read-only page of level B is "no write" at stage B; the levels and the root of a stage that the entry does not use are not examined; the root of a used stage must be page aligned and below 2^40; the leaf of a device is a page of level 0, 1 or 2 only, whatever the number of levels; a table (device table, ring, level A or B) must be in RAM; the model discards exactly what a command names (and everything if the cache holds 4096 translations) and does not discard more, so that a wrong-tag command is seen to leave an entry; the one refusal of a transfer is logged once (a refused access is sent to a region of the unit that answers with an error, because QEMU's own "unassigned" region takes only four bytes at a time and would have translated and logged an eight-byte transfer twice); a transfer that crosses a page and is refused is refused once for each page it touches, as the transfers of real links are split. *8. What is not done, and cannot be tested in the model:* the hypervisor-mode path (the model has no hypervisor mode: the level-A and level-B roles are played by the test program; there is no virtual unit and no forwarding of a level-A fault to a guest's virtual log, no guest, no world switch); real guests; `sim64` has no bus (there is no IOMMU or PCI Express in `sim64` and none is planned there); the guard that blocks a device whose guest is being invalidated is a flag held while the invalidation runs, which the synchronous executor never lets a device see, so it has no test; "invalidate device does not complete before the device's requests in flight are finished or refused" holds trivially (QEMU's device DMA is synchronous) and has no test; translation requests (2.11.9): a request that arrives as already translated is refused with the cause "translation request" (a second IOMMU index), and nothing in QEMU's models sends one, so it has no test; the unmap notifications to QEMU's notifier list are sent on every discard and nothing registers a notifier; interrupts are the four pins and one wired line, no MSI (J.6.3); no `virtio` device is used with the unit (`iommu_platform=on` is not tried); a device behind a bridge is keyed by the identity QEMU gives it (the bus is read when the access is made); the group rule (2.11.5) is a property of third-party switches and is not modelled; what a real device does with an error completion is unknown to the model. *Records:* `docs/implementation-status.md` (the IOMMU entry), `qemu/README.md`, `spec/README.md`, the ledger (the virtualisation section), `handoff.md` item 1 of section 7, R517.
195. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-19; R518): the 23 decisions of the design of the hypervisor mode are accepted as recommended.** The owner answered the form of `docs/encoding-work/hypervisor-design.md` section 10: "Accept my recommendation" for each of the 23, with no comments. The design is therefore the specification of 6.14.3 to 6.14.5, 6.14.8 and 6.14.11 to 6.14.14: one internal state bit `H` outside the status word and a machine strap `HVE` (with `HVE` clear the machine is today's machine); the hypervisor register block at system-register numbers 128 to 147 (`HTRAP` with its hypercall mask, `HEXITR`, `HEPC`, `HEPSW`, `HEVA`, `HCAUSE`, `HGPA`, `HINSN`, `HVBR`, `GTBR`, `VIRQ`, `VIVEC`, `VTOFF`, `VCPUID`, `GEVA`, `GECAUSE`, `GKSP`, `GKDP`, `GSDP`, `GUDP`); a fourth bank of `R14` and `DP`; exit through a vector table at `HVBR + 8 * HCAUSE` with the new vector numbers 17 to 20; stage 2 in the page-table entry format with a two-page root and software accessed and dirty bits; the rules of invalidation by guest tag; one new mnemonic, `GENTER`, in the reserved system word `syspg2`, and no guest-return instruction; seven virtual interrupt lines and a core-local virtual timer page; the table of the state after a guest reset; the guest reset as an operation of the hypervisor. *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing of it is in the manual body (the statements of 6.14 still say "details to be determined"), the grammar, `as64`, `sim64`, QEMU or LLVM; the implementation follows (errata 196), and the manual text of the registers, the exit codes and the sequences will be shown to the owner before it is written. *Open in the design and not decided by this answer:* the facts that the design lists as unverified (memory-type combining across the two stages; whether QEMU can present a core-local timer page; the physical-interrupt tests in sim64).
196. **DONE (the owner's instruction, 2026-11-19; R519): the hypervisor mode of `docs/encoding-work/hypervisor-design.md` is implemented in the shared executor, `sim64` and the QEMU target, in four steps, each with tests that were checked against scratch mutations of the model.** The owner's words: "do it all", in answer to the offer to implement the 23 accepted decisions (errata 195). The specification is the design document; where it and the code or the grammar disagreed, the document's rule was taken (see *Drift*). *1. The state and the registers (commit 1):* the internal state bit `H` and the machine strap `HVE` (`sim64 -H`, which implies `-K`; QEMU `-M pdp64-virt,kernel-mode=on,hypervisor=on`); with `HVE` clear the machine is today's machine (the registers and `GENTER` are illegal instructions, the new `hv-off` test checks all 20 registers by a patched word and no existing test changed); with it set the core starts at `H` = 1 with `HTRAP` = `WFE` and `SEV`. The register block, numbers 128 to 147 (`HTRAP`, `HEXITR`, `HEPC`, `HEPSW`, `HEVA`, `HCAUSE`, `HGPA`, `HINSN`, `HVBR`, `GTBR`, `VIRQ`, `VIVEC`, `VTOFF`, `VCPUID`, `GEVA`, `GECAUSE`, `GKSP`, `GKDP`, `GSDP`, `GUDP`) with the design's widths (a write of a one to a reserved bit of `HTRAP` is an illegal instruction; the others mask), read-only rules (`HEXITR`, `HEVA`, `HCAUSE`, `HGPA`, `HINSN`: the assembler refuses a write, the executor too), a fourth bank of `R14` and `DP`, the vector numbers 17 to 20, the exit codes and the trap-control bits (new grammar part `52-hypervisor-v1`, `vector` in `50-system-v1`, the names in `41-sysreg`: the assembler, the 12-bit field of `MFSR` and `MTSR` and the generated tables take numbers up to 147 with no change). **`GENTER` took the reserved system word `syspg2`** (the word is `fbc8`; renamed in `20-enc-forms`, `80-mn-ext`, the mirror `cat.py`, the facts, the hand group `mn-sysx` numbers it 6; `addforms.py` writes the generated parts back byte for byte; the diff of `spec/pdp64/tables.md` is the one rename; no leaf added, no pattern moved, the optimiser was not run; `syspg3` to `syspg5` stay reserved and `tests/sim/unimpl-syspg2` became `unimpl-syspg3`). Semantics: privileged to `H` = 1; at `H` = 0 an exit (code 18) after the guest's own privilege check; a reserved mode in `HEPSW` is an illegal instruction; it includes an `ISYNC`, switches the banks, clears the naming of the vector accumulators. The exits are in `common/pdp64exec.c`: `gtrap` (the privilege check first, then the bit of `HTRAP`) on `WFE`, `SEV`, `HINT #1`, `SPL` and a changing `MTPS`, `TLBI`, `TLBIA`, `TLBSYNC`, `MTASID`, `MFASID`, `MTSR` and `MFSR` of `ASID`, writes of `PTBR`, `MMUCTL` and `VBR`, `MFCPUID` and `MFSR CPUID` (without the bit a guest reads `VCPUID`), `ICINV`, `ICINVA`, `BPT`, the single step; `gexit` for the events that always exit (`HALT`, `MTDMW`, `MTSR` and `MFSR` of `DMW0` and `DMW1`, any hypervisor register or `GENTER` used by a guest); `TRAP #n` routed by the 8-bit mask at any guest mode (vector 256 + n); a physical interrupt always exits (code 1, `HCAUSE` the vector, `HEPC` the next instruction); a vector fetch that fails is a machine check exit (code 21). An exit writes `HEPC`, `HEPSW`, `HEVA`, `HCAUSE`, `HGPA`, `HINSN`, `HEXITR` and enters at `HVBR` + 8 * `HCAUSE` at priority 7 with the guest's mode as the previous mode; `RTI`, `RTT`, `PUSHX` and `POPX` name `HEPC` and `HEPSW` at `H` = 1; the hypervisor is untranslated. *2. Virtual interrupts and the virtual timer (commit 2):* seven lines, delivery as the guest's own exception entry with the vector of the line in `VIVEC`, the line cleared by the hardware, the guest's `PRI`, `SPL` and `MTPS` masking only these (no exit), the physical interrupt first, a masked line ends a `WFE`, no delivery at `H` = 1; the QEMU CPU kicks the translation loop after `VIRQ`, `GENTER`, `SPL`; the virtual timer page `MAP_VTIMER` (the registers of the timer; counter = real counter minus `VTOFF`; expiry sets `VIRQ` bit 6 once per arming; a write to `VTOFF` moves the deadline through a new optional hook of the executor). *3. Stage 2 (commit 3):* `GTBR` with the enable bit, the root and the 16-bit guest tag; the walk (page-table entry format, `U` and `G` zero, three levels, a root of two pages, software accessed and dirty bits, the guest's table reads and its output both through it, rights intersected, the smaller page); a failure is an exit (19 invalid, 20 refused; vectors 18, 19, 20) with the record of the design (`HEPC` the instruction, `HEVA`, `HGPA`, `HINSN`, flags `WALK` and `VEC`); the guest's vector fetch through stage 2 (a miss exits with nothing changed: `HEPC` the instruction, or the interrupted one, so the exception is taken after the fill; a virtual line stays pending); `PROBER`/`PROBEW` test both stages (a stage-2 miss exits, a stage-1 miss gives 0); QEMU MMU indexes 4 to 7; the architectural cache keyed by (tag, ASID, mode index, page) beside QEMU's own TLB, discarded only as G-a to G-d say (new executor ops `PDP64_TLB_SYNC` and `PDP64_TLB_GTBR`: `GENTER`, `ISYNC` and a change of the tag alone discard nothing). *4. The first software (commit 4):* `tests/qemu/hv-first.S`: a hypervisor of our own and one guest kernel (its own vector table and page tables, a trapped `MTSR PTBR` emulated at `H` = 1, a demand-mapped stage-2 page, a virtual timer interrupt through the timer page mapped in its stage 2, a user program whose system call does not exit and prints by a routed `TRAP #224`, `HALT` as the end), output and counters checked. *Tests:* `tests/sim/hv-regs` (reset values, widths, read-only, aliases, banks, unassigned numbers by a patched word, the hypervisor's own exception, `PUSHX`/`POPX`), `hv-switch` (status word and banks in and out, a reserved mode, the accumulator clear at the exit and at `GENTER`), `hv-exits` (generated by `gen_hv.py`: 194 exits from 127 cases, every bit set and clear, every always-exit event, the hypercall mask, user mode and the privilege check first, unassigned numbers), `hv-off`, `hv-irq` (`sim64 -i`), `hv-virq` and `hv-virq-irq`, `tests/qemu/hv-timer`, `hv-vtimer`, `hv-stage2` (generated by `gen_hv_stage2.py`: 138 checks) and `hv-first`; the `sim` tests run on QEMU too (`tools/check-qemu.sh` maps `-H`); `tests/asm` `syshv` (words computed by hand), `sysua`, `tests/asm-err` (a write to each read-only register, `GENTER` with an operand); `tests/exectest-enc.c` now runs every decoded word on a machine with the strap (a word the machine without it calls illegal would otherwise fail it: `hv-off` covers that case). *Non-vacuity (scratch edits of the executor and of the QEMU target, each made the named test fail):* executor: `HALT` not exiting, `WFE` not trapped, the privilege check after the trap bit, a guest's hypervisor-register access not exiting, `GENTER` at `H` = 0 not exiting, the vector accumulator clear removed at the exit and at `GENTER`, the virtual core number not returned, no bank switch, a physical interrupt masked by the guest's priority or not first, the hypercall mask index, the reserved bit of `HTRAP`, `SEV`, `HINT`, `MTPS`, `BPT`, the single step, `TRAP` at user mode, the direct-mapping window, `RTI` at `H` = 1, the `GEVA` alias, the virtual line priority, order, hardware clear and vector rule; QEMU: the virtual timer without `VTOFF` and without its interrupt, the architectural cache not discarded by `TLBIA` or by `TLBI`, ignoring the tag, no discard at a write to `GTBR`, the final stage-2 translation, the stage-2 reads of the guest's walk, the stage-2 vector fetch, the dirty bit, the `U` bit, the probe exit. *Drift between the design and the code, and what was taken:* the design names `syspg2` for `GENTER` after errata 192 took `syspg0` and `syspg1` (taken); `HEXITR` flags and codes are those of 1.6; the manual body (J.9.6 and A.1.2) still says four reserved system words and is not edited here (the `SYSPG` entry and its story, which are generated, now say three). *Differences between the model and the design (also in `docs/implementation-status.md`):* `GTBR` and the other registers mask reserved bits silently (only `HTRAP` refuses a one); `VIRQ` bit 0 reads zero; a virtual line whose `VIVEC` field is below 32 is not delivered and stays pending (the design says 32 to 255 are legal and says nothing of the rest); the hypervisor is privileged whatever the mode field says; the stage-2 leaf is checked against the space bit as stage 1 is (errata 65; the design left the combining of memory types open); a bus error on a stage-2 table is reported as an invalid entry, not as code 21; the vector fetch of an instruction's own entry (`TRAP`, `BPT`, a check trap) that stage 2 refuses exits with `HEPC` at the instruction; the architectural cache also keys the mode index and `PDP64_TLB_ALL` (a write to `PTBR`, `ASID`, `MMUCTL`) discards every entry of the tag (more than 6.8.4 asks: allowed); the model does not count the reads of the nested walk (not required); the virtual timer page is a device of the bus at a provisional address (`MAP_VTIMER`), one core, so it is the core's own (QEMU can give each core its own through the `memory` link of the CPU: not built, not testable with one core); `HVE` needs the kernel-mode machine. *Not done:* the manual's statements of 6.14 (the owner sees the proposed text first; the manual describes the architecture as it is to be; the generated instruction entry for `GENTER` and the dictionary are in); the guest reset sequence of 2.6 and a row by row test of the reset table of section 4 (the machine-reset values are tested; the guest reset is an operation of the hypervisor and has no test); the clear of an `LL` reservation (there are no atomics in the executor); two guests and a world switch between them in the first software (one guest kernel was asked; the tag is tested in `hv-stage2`); `sim64` has no MMU, so stage 2 and the tag are QEMU only; nothing of the IOMMU path of the hypervisor (no virtual unit); `llvm/check.sh` was run once after step 1 (the LLVM tables changed by the one word; 61 `.ll` and 88 vector programs, all pass) and the tables were not touched afterwards; `tools/strip-equiv.py` was not run (the vector executor was not changed). *Record:* the pcc names header (`../pcc/arch/pdp64/pdp64-names.h`, written by `tools/export-pcc-headers.sh`) changed and needs committing on both pcc branches (not committed here). *Records:* `docs/implementation-status.md`, the ledger (the virtualisation section), `handoff.md` item 1 of section 7, R519.
197. **CORRECTION (the assistant's, 2026-11-19; R520; no new decision): the reserved system words that are left are three.** There were six (`syspg0` to `syspg5`); `stua` and `clua` took `syspg0` and `syspg1` (errata 192) and `genter` took `syspg2` (errata 196), so `syspg3`, `syspg4` and `syspg5` are left. The generated entry `SYSPG` (9.14) says three since errata 196; A.1.2 and J.9.6 (its title, its second paragraph, option 2 which now names `SYSPG3`, and option 4) said four and now say three. The decision form `docs/feedback/decisions-feedback.txt` was regenerated for the new title.
198. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-19; R521): a design principle: start from what other architectures do, amend minimally so that holes close by construction, and propose that way.** The owner's words: "another principle I want is: do what other architectures do, or something close to it, except where it contradicts our principles, then make a minimal change of semantics or instruction set so the hole or problem closes by construction.  we want to constructively solve problems rather than post hoc. just like the openbsd principles. so all those vector, mmu, iommu virtualization questions etc.... make your proposals fit our principles, and start with "best of breed designs" with corrections/amendments unless a bold change would really be a big win overall, we are open to those too." *Applies to:* every proposal from now on, and the open questions of the vector block (the hidden state, the naming of the accumulators, the strip), the MMU and the translation buffer, the IOMMU, the interrupt controller, the atomics and virtualization. *The method it implies:* for each question, first the best-of-breed design of the other architectures (read in their own documentation or sources), then the amendments that the pdp64's principles require, each a minimal change of semantics or instruction set; and a bold change is proposed where it would be a large gain overall. *Not done, stated plainly:* the decisions that were made before this principle (errata 172, 181, 186, 187, 190, 195) are not reopened by it; the reviews that apply it to them are being written (`docs/encoding-work/best-of-breed-*.md`) and anything they propose against a recorded decision will be put to the owner as a question.
199. **DECISIONS AND QUESTIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-19; R522): seL4 is a design target; 8 of the 14 decisions of the seL4 review are accepted, 1 and 2 are not decided, and 4, 6, 12 and 14 are questions.** The owner's answers are in R522 (his words, including his notes). *Accepted as recommended in `docs/encoding-work/sel4-fit.md` section 8:* 3 the atomics (fix the operand layout and the `SC` result, implement them, run litmus tests before a second core exists); 5 a formal model of the kernel-relevant subset (the owner: rules are directional, not iron-clad, so "nothing written by hand twice" may bend); 7 the system-call convention (capability address `R0`, message info `R1`, message registers `R2` to `R5`, `R6` and `R7` for the mixed-criticality extension, the syscall number in the `TRAP` number, the IPC buffer in user `DP`); 8 one statement that the hypervisor level has its own translation roots and tag, the guest tag at least 16 bits, no more design until the non-hypervisor kernel runs; 9 block operations (for a single observer a loop in ascending order with its progress in the registers; 7.5.8 unchanged for concurrent observers; not emitted in the verified kernel if they cannot be modelled); 10 `KTBR` as system register 13 with a one-page root and J.6.6 decided as \"the kernel maps itself at its physical address first\"; 11 the wording of 9.2.6 (the owner's \"except the mov instructions, eh?\" is not understood and is to be clarified); 13 `UA` and `.U` kept. *seL4 itself:* the owner: \"seL4 is already done for arm and riscv; yes we definitely will want seL4 for pdp64\"; which configuration first is not decided. *Not decided:* 2 (the smallest interrupt controller now, the remapping unit later) pending the review of interrupts (`docs/encoding-work/best-of-breed-iommu-irq-atomics.md`). *Questions the owner asked and which are answered in conversation, not decided:* 4 (invalidate by ASID, `TLBIAS`), 6 (finishing the instructions the executor lacks), 12 (the toolchain and CompCert), 14 (the check instructions in the verified build); the owner's idea of a core-number register and a page of special registers (on 3) is a proposal to be worked out under the principle of errata 198; the estimate of the gates and the work of nested guests that he asked for on 8 is being written (`docs/encoding-work/nested-virtualization-estimate.md`). *Not done, stated plainly:* none of it is implemented or written into the manual.
200. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-19; R523): a vector block resumes where possible; and every security hole and information bleed must be prevented, and be in the assembler test corpus.** The owner's words: "push the pdp64 and pcc commits; i choose to resume where possible, especially since it only costs a few gates, i just want it known that all security holes, information bleeds must be dealt with and prevented, even if only to have it in the corpus of assembler to test that the implementation doesn't have these holes." *Two decisions:* (1) **resume where possible.** After the review `docs/encoding-work/best-of-breed-vector.md` (which measured the work that restart-from-entry loses and recommended keeping it for bounded blocks, with resume as the bold alternative) and the owner's note on the `VCLR` form ("this rule of no hidden state crosses an exception.... are there cases where it is safe if it does? if something has a lot of state, wouldn't resume give a real savings? bench this. hidden register files etc, what is their cost?"), the owner chooses resume, for its small cost in gates. This supersedes in part the choice of R500 (errata 172, 173, 186: restart from the entry) and 6.14.9 for the cases where resume is possible; the design of the resume (what state is saved or architectural, the dirty state, the strip, the hypervisor and world switch, the in-place block, the naming of the accumulators, which blocks cannot resume) is to be written under the principle of errata 198 and shown to the owner; his answer on the `VCLR` form (option 1: the naming only inside the block) is to be reconciled in that design. (2) **the security rule.** Every security hole and every information bleed found, in this design or in the implementation, is to be dealt with and prevented; and each is to have a test in the corpus of assembly programs, so that an implementation can be tested for not having it, even where the architecture already prevents it. *Applies to:* the audit's findings, the hidden vector state, the stale translations, the interrupt and DMA paths, the world switch, and every hole of the reviews of errata 198; the corpus is a standing requirement. *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing of resume is implemented or written into the manual; the corpus does not exist as such yet (some tests are in `tests/sim` and `tests/qemu`, for example the probes of the audit); the design of resume and the corpus are being written.
201. **DONE (the owner's rule of errata 200, 2026-11-19; R524): the security corpus exists: 68 holes and information bleeds indexed in `docs/security-corpus.md`, 17 new probe programs, an index, a runner, and the current implementation run against all of it; seven probes find a hole.** *The owner's words (errata 200):* "all security holes, information bleeds must be dealt with and prevented, even if only to have it in the corpus of assembler to test that the implementation doesn't have these holes." *What the corpus is:* a probe is a small assembly program that looks for one hole and exits 0 if the implementation does not have it, otherwise with the number of the first failed check; every probe has a control. The holes were taken from the sensitive-instruction audit, the four best-of-breed reviews, the nested-virtualization estimate, the seL4 review, the hardware-vulnerabilities checklist and the earlier errata on stale translations, the user-access guard and the IOMMU. *How it is organised:* the probes that pass are tests (`tests/sim/sec-*`, run on sim64 and QEMU; `tests/qemu/sec-*`, QEMU only) and are run by `tools/check-sim64.sh`, `tools/check-qemu.sh` and `tools/check-all.sh`; the probes that find a hole are in `tests/sec-open/sim` and `tests/sec-open/qemu` and are run by the new `tools/check-sec.sh`, which reports FOUND, PASS or BROKEN for each and exits 0 (`--check`, run by `tools/check-all.sh`, also fails if the index and the findings disagree, if a probe is not in the index or an index row names a missing file). The regular probes are written by `tests/sec/gen_sec.py`; the rows that name earlier tests (41 of them) index them and do not copy them. *Result on the current implementation:* 68 holes: 42 pass, 7 FOUND, 19 N/A. **FOUND:** (1) a guest reset that only rewrites stage 2 and enters the guest again leaves the guest's cached translations (writing MMUCTL as 0 first does discard them, and passes): the leak of the Linux CVE of errata 178, left to the hypervisor's TLBIA (`sec-hv-reset-inplace`, QEMU); (2) a device whose table maps a device address onto the timer's page writes the timer's compare register by DMA: the unit checks read and write, not the target (`sec-dma-devspace`, QEMU); (3) an exception between VCLR and VEC changes the result of the program (`sec-vec-clear-interrupt`); (4) an accumulator block that updates memory in place is wrong after a page fault, 4 of 8 lanes 2 instead of 1 (`sec-vec-inplace`); (5) the hidden record of an aborted block is found by the address of the VEC and not by the context, so another context at the same address inherits the accumulator naming (`sec-vec-stale-record`); (6) the kernel's scalar registers r2 to r13 are visible to the user after the return (`sec-regs-carry`) and (7) the floating-point flags carry from one context to the next (`sec-fpsr-carry`): these two are by design (only R14 and DP are banked; the audit calls fpsr a software swap) and are the owner's to decide. **N/A, and why:** timing, caches, speculation, memory chips and power (the model has none; the architectural rule that a faulting load yields no data is tested); SEV, the event state and broadcast invalidates (one core); atomics and reservations, nesting, the second root register, a hypervisor translation regime, the interrupt-message path, the memory-type combining (not built, not decided or no data cache); W^X (the page bits allow it, enforcing it is policy); a block longer than the interrupt interval (the executor runs a block whole); the IOMMU's in-flight requests, table visibility, translated requests, peer-to-peer traffic, coherence and device atomics (the model's bus is synchronous and has none of them). *Non-vacuity:* each passing probe was run against a scratch copy of the model broken the way the hole would break it and failed (table in `docs/security-corpus.md`); the finding probes were shown able to pass by the change that closes the hole. It caught two mistakes of the corpus itself (a scratch register compared with itself; flags read after instructions that change them), both fixed. *Not done, stated plainly:* no hole was fixed (the owner decides each); the design of resume is another document; the probe for a fault in the final strip of an accumulator block is the same cause as (4) and is not a separate program; the corpus is a standing requirement and grows with each hole found.
202. **ANSWERS (the owner's, 2026-11-19; R525): the forms of the vector and MMU reviews are answered, with many questions; the primary principles are restated.** The owner's answers and notes are in R525, in his words. *The owner restated the primary principles* (on the first decision of the vector form: "let's correct the premise; i'm ok with major changes from common or standard practice, but it must be in line with the primary principles I outlined as the basis for this pdp64 project. safety faster and fewer instructions (although not necessarily a smaller instruction set, still it would be nice as the final principle after the others are met, is a coherent consistent set of instructions that a programmer can keep in their head as they could the pdp-11."): in order, safety, speed, fewer instructions, and last a coherent set that a programmer can keep in their head; a departure from the common practice is welcome when it serves them. *Also:* "stop with all these numbered references when a few words can explain the issue" (the reviews and forms are to explain in words). *Vector review:* decisions 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 accepted, 1 not decided, 2 unanswered; the owner asked for a proper benchmark, for gates and for speed, of the bound on an accumulator block ("if a 256 byte restart slows us down, I want to know"); the choice of errata 200 (resume where possible) takes precedence and the design of resume (`docs/encoding-work/vector-resume-design.md`, being written) is to answer these questions. *MMU review:* decisions 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 9, 10, 11, 12 and 13 accepted (4 and 6 with questions), 1, 5 and 8 unanswered (questions; and on 8 the owner: "mnemonics and generator runs are cheap; just make this correct, safe, efficient"). *IOMMU, interrupts and atomics review:* the form was cancelled; nothing is decided from it. *Not done, stated plainly:* none of it is implemented or written into the manual; the questions are answered in conversation, and the answers are not recorded here.
203. **ANSWERS (the owner's, 2026-11-19; R526): the 5 questions on the 7 holes found by the security corpus (errata 201).** The owner's answers are in R526 in his words. *Do as recommended:* hole 1 (a guest reset in place leaves old translations: make a guest reset one obvious write; the owner asks \"is there something we can do so software doesn't even have to do it at all?\"); hole 2 (DMA into another device's registers: the IOMMU must refuse; the owner asks whether one device may DMA into another that it has permission for, the CPU staying the final arbiter); holes 3 to 5 (the vector ones: closed by `VCLR` only inside the block and the resume design; the owner: \"yeah if the interrupt would change the result, then should be cleared anyway, no point trying to resume right?\"). *No choice, questions:* hole 6 (the kernel's registers visible to the user after a return: \"flash clear sounds interesting.... bench it. i love protecting us from careless kernels.\") and hole 7 (the floating-point flags: \"what is the cost of doing it in hardware? again, love protecting us from careless kernels.\"). *Under way:* a study with measured gates and cycles of the ways to close holes 1, 6 and 7 by construction (`docs/encoding-work/closing-holes-by-construction.md`). *Not done, stated plainly:* none of it is implemented; the corpus keeps the probes that found the holes.
204. **DECISIONS AND INSTRUCTIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R527): the answers of `answers01.txt`.** The owner's words are in R527 (the whole file). *Decided:* (1) **the method:** "change what our principles require and if there are some big juicy easy wins to be had by changing from the standard, then do so. as long as we can support it in the compilers etc."; (2) **the hypervisor level is Arm VHE-style at once** ("do arm style immediately so kernels don't need to be split in two"), so the kernel of the hypervisor level has its own translation and uses the ordinary registers; (3) **`TLBIAS`** (invalidate by address-space number) approved; (4) **the memory type of the two stages: the stricter category wins**; (5) **the device-space bit is gone and the platform map is approved** ("i thought that was already what an iommu does"); (6) **`TLBI` by guest-physical page is a separate mnemonic**; (7) **finish all the missing instructions of the executor** (the 46 of `spec/facts/core.toml`; the owner asks why they are missing and which); (8) **seL4: the check instructions are not emitted in the verified kernel** ("that's the whole point of proof. checks are for what we can't prove"); item 11 of the seL4 form is ignored ("brain fart"); (9) **the 256-strip bound on a block and resume are accepted** ("both are clear wins. do them. accepted") with `VCLR` only inside a block; (10) **`RTI #mask`** approved (a return carries a mask of registers to keep, the hardware clears the others), and the same on the guest entry; the owner leans to hardware clearing or stashing and restoring on the return "according to a setting the kernel sets, and whichever is most efficient in compiled code fewest instructions overall", to be benchmarked; (11) **the floating-point flags are cleared when the address-space number, the guest register or the page-table base is written** (+7 gates, no cycles): approved; (12) **the 7 holes of the security corpus are to be fixed**; hole 2: "why on earth wouldn't iommu register pages and other device register pages not be protected by default? i agree with your rule about system registers and peer devices". *Instructions:* verify the table-coherence behaviour of x86, Arm and IBM, the HATRIC paper and the hypervisor cycle figures ("no stone unturned! save all bench results and answers to queries!"); bench the hardware that notices stores to page-table pages against the arena (hole 1) and the three alternatives of hole 6; send the 17-point hypervisor text form with full explanations and pros and cons; update all forms in the light of the answers and send them one at a time. *Questions the owner asked* (answered in conversation and saved in `docs/owner-questions.md`): the rule of the main loop; the vfin/vfmab hidden-state case (to be fixed); a sequential token for each guest; interrupts inside a block; the arena against the directory of walked table pages; the hole of the returned registers; what the floating-point flags are; why the 46 instructions are missing and which. *Not done, stated plainly:* none of the decisions is implemented yet; the implementations follow in the repositories pdp64, qemu-pdp64 and llvm-pdp64.
205. **ANSWERS (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R528): the 15 points of the hypervisor manual text.** The answers are in R528 in his words. *Accepted as recommended:* unused bit 0 of the virtual lines reads zero; a virtual line with a vector below 32 is not delivered (changed by the owner: see below); the hypervisor may execute every privileged instruction; the virtual timer's alarm fires once each time it is armed; no virtual line is delivered while the hypervisor runs; a stage-2 fault on the vector fetch of an instruction's own trap reports the instruction (it is retried); a bus error while reading a stage-2 table is a machine check, code 21, as designed (the owner asks what the codes are and how the broken-pointer ambiguity is resolved: answered in conversation, to be settled in the design of the exit codes); the hypervisor's vector table base is page aligned; a reserved mode value in the saved status word is refused when used; the kernel stack register after a reset is set by the platform's boot protocol; the reset-table row of the virtual timer is kept; the text says `TRAP` and `TRAPL`; the saved status word's width is not given as a number. *Changed by the owner:* an unused bit of a hypervisor register: **refuse a write of a one** (in every register, not only the trap-control register); a virtual line vector below 32: **refuse the write at once**. *The owner's request:* "yeah we need to define those timer registers help me out please, best practice and best possible improved versions." (a design of the timer registers, `docs/encoding-work/timer-design.md`, is being written). *Not done, stated plainly:* the draft of the manual text is being revised for these answers and for the decisions of errata 204; nothing is written into the manual; the model and the executor still do what they did (read as zero, delivered never).
206. **DECISION AND INSTRUCTION (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R529): the stage-2 table bus error is split by what the bus says; the hypervisor text is to be rewritten to lead with the story.** The owner's words are in R529. *Decided:* (1) "split 19 and 21 by what the bus says": a bus error while reading a stage-2 table is reported as an invalid entry (code 19, with a flag that the entry points at nothing) when no device answered at the address, and as a machine check (code 21) when a device answered with an error. *Question* (answered in conversation and in `docs/owner-questions.md`): whether the flush bit guarantees coherence and would make the pdp64 the first and only. *Instruction:* the owner found the hypervisor text "the densest most boring prose i've ever seen" and wants it to "lead with the story a pdp11 programmer would understand. motivate it. what is a hypervisor, who did it first, how does it work in broad brush strokes, then get into the intermediate level, then finally the fine details." The manual text of the hypervisor mode (`docs/encoding-work/proposed-manual-text-hv.md`, a draft) is therefore being rewritten in that order (`docs/encoding-work/hypervisor-text-v2.md`) and will be shown to the owner before anything is written into the manual. *Not done, stated plainly:* the manual's 6.14 is unchanged; the executor and the model do not yet split the code.
207. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R530): the holes form after the benchmarks.** Accepted as recommended in `docs/encoding-work/bench-holes-1-and-6.md`: (1) the arena and its flag close hole 1 in the first version; (3) flush at every guest entry is the default when no arena is set; (4) the walker refuses a stage-2 table outside the arena; (6) a guest never sees a stale translation of a changed stage-2 table, and the two checks of `tests/qemu/hv-stage2.S` that assert the opposite are rewritten ("we don't give a shit about the old behavior"); (7) the flush bit in the guest register is dropped; (8) `RTI #mask` is the only return to a lesser mode, the hardware clearing the registers outside the mask; (9) no mask in a system register; (10) flash clear; (11) no hardware stash and restore; (12) the same mask on `GENTER`, and the callee-saved set and the status of `R16` to `R23` written into the ABI. *Asked again, because the answer is ambiguous:* (2) the directory of walked table pages (the recommendation was "no for now" but the comment says "do it"); (5) stores by other cores and devices (the answer was the other way with no comment). *Benchmark requested:* how often a return would use the same mask as its `GENTER`, and whether `RTI` could reuse the mask its `GENTER` set. *Standing instructions for the manual text:* undecided matters are documented outside the manual, so the manual carries no *(D)* or other status marks and no pending-detail notes; and since 1.0 is not released the manual text carries no references to errata. They apply to the new hypervisor text now (`docs/encoding-work/hypervisor-text-v2.md`); the rest of the manual still carries them and is not yet changed. *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is implemented.
208. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R531): ten further design principles approved, and the order of all of them.** The owner's words are in R531; the principles, their order and the evidence are in `docs/encoding-work/principles-approved.md`. *Decided:* (1) the ten principles derived from the owner's own words (prevent then detect then recover; hardware, not discipline; one binary on every implementation; measure, do not guess; frequency-weighted encoding; rules are directional; one source of truth, generated; Occam; standard at the edges; peek-and-poke pleasure) are approved with the order the assistant predicted; (2) *speed above fewer instructions, speed as measured by actual run time;* fewer instructions executed and fewer fetches are strong proxies, but only proxies; this reverses the order in errata 83, which ranked fewer instructions executed above speed; (3) the ladder of safety, in the owner's words: prevent by construction (hardware), prevent by idiom (software), mitigate side effects, minimize the cost of failure and recovery; (4) from the same day (R531's context): prior work already done and extra work to implement or document are never reasons for or against an option. All pending decisions are to be judged again against the full set before any is taken. *Not done, stated plainly:* the earlier recommendations are not adopted; no decision of the 57 pending is taken yet.
209. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R532): the complete set of design principles approved, in order.** The owner edited `docs/encoding-work/principles-complete-for-review.md` and wrote "approved"; that file is now `docs/encoding-work/principles-approved.md` and replaces the thirteen-item file of errata 208, which had left out the owner's earlier principles. *Decided:* six goals in this order: (1) safety and security by the ladder (prevent by construction in hardware; prevent by idiom in software; mitigate side effects; minimize the cost of failure and recovery), which includes no strange side effects, hardware not discipline, every hole and bleed prevented and tested in the corpus, **make it easy and low cost to be correct (to write correct code)**, and **hardware that encourages software flaws is changed**; (2) speed measured by actual run time, and power, which a low gate count serves; (3) fewer instructions executed and fewer fetches, strong proxies for speed and only proxies; (4) dense encoding; (5) a good compiler target; (6) pleasant in assembler, which is the same thing as a coherent set a programmer can keep in their head. *Standing constraints and measures, whatever the order:* one binary on every implementation (a veto); measure, do not guess; hot-loop weight; rules are soft; Occam; prior work and the effort of a change are never a reason. *Method:* best of breed first, amended minimally, and do not be afraid to revive ideas and practices of the past, even the 1960s; learn from the past's flaws and mistakes in hardware and software and dodge them all; one source of truth, generated; standard at the edges with no wrapper without a justification; verify before stating. *Working rules* (not design principles): explain everything to the old PDP-11 assembler and Unix programmer asleep for fifty years; plain words, story first; first person manual built with buildabook and checked after every change; do not overwrite before verifying; save every benchmark and answer; push only when told. This supersedes the order in errata 83 (where fewer instructions executed ranked above speed) and completes errata 208. *Not done, stated plainly:* every pending decision is to be judged again against this set before any is taken; none is taken yet.
210. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R533): the re-judged recommendations are taken.** After the complete principles of errata 209 were approved, the pending decisions, the decisions already taken and the recommendations the owner had accepted were judged again against them; the owner wrote "accept all five, then take the rest". *The five that reverse or reopen an earlier decision, accepted:* (1) the guest's timer line 6 is a **level**, like the real one (this reverses the owner's answer of 2026-11-25 that the alarm fires once each time it is armed); (2) **there is no short `RTI`:** the mask form is the only `RTI`, so a plain return to a lesser mode does not exist; (3) **stores by other cores and devices are handled now,** not when the multi-core design is written: a device never writes the arena (the IOMMU refuses), and cores are covered by an epoch word in memory; (4) the question of **full capability pointers** is reopened and is to be decided on a measurement of run time and memory growth; (5) the **accumulator-block strip** (a fixed 16 bytes) and the **wave-1 removals** (the tile, the whole-GEMM unit, the gather) are reopened and are to be decided on run-time measurements of inference and sparse kernels. *"The rest":* every other recommendation in `docs/encoding-work/rejudge-timer-iommu-irq-atomics.md` (T1 to T12, F1 to F21: among them the deadline register's zero means never, no unprivileged clock in version 1 and the kernel-updated time page withdrawn, the interrupt controller's core side as system registers and the page for devices only, the fixed 128-byte `LL`/`SC` bound, the IOMMU reading its tables coherently, the atomics layout of three registers, 4 and 8 byte sizes, precise fault on misaligned and uncached atomics, sequential consistency kept), `docs/encoding-work/rejudge-holes-rti-resume-hv.md` (A1 to A12, B1 to B5, C0 to C15, D1 to D11, E1 to E19: among them the arena only for now, the mask in the instruction with the added mask bit for the vector registers and the floating-point flags, resume justified on five conditions with the rule that resume is optional, the keyed per-guest token, the hypervisor's ordinary register names meaning its own, a one-page stage-2 root, the hardware-allocated guest tag of fixed width 16), `docs/encoding-work/rejudge-decided-items.md` and `docs/encoding-work/rejudge-accepted-recommendations.md` (the changes they recommend; the items marked "needs measurement" are decided when the measurement is made, and are listed there). *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is implemented yet; the measurements named in those files do not exist yet; the manual text is not yet changed.
211. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R534): the proposals of the hypervisor text v3 are taken, and atomics and memory-model examples are to be added.** The owner's words are in R534. *Taken, as proposed in `docs/encoding-work/hypervisor-text-v3.md` (list 3 and the preface):* (1) the guest tag is allocated by the hardware, 16 bits, **with a generation count**: when the counter wraps the hardware flushes the whole translation buffer and bumps the generation, and a guest whose tag is from an older generation is given a fresh tag at its next `GENTER` (this closes the hole that a guest outliving a full turn of the counter could be given a number still live); (2) the ordinary register names at the hypervisor level mean the hypervisor level's own registers; there is no fourth bank in the sense of a visible `R14` and `DP` copy that a guest's names reach, and where storage for the hypervisor's own stack and direct-page registers is needed it is the hypervisor level's own copy; the guest's registers are reached by explicit `G` names, numbers 149 to 164, copies of all twelve; (3) the field layouts of `HARENA`, `HEPOCH` and `GTOKEN`; `FEATURES` as system register 32; the mnemonic `TLBIGPA`; exit codes 22 and 23 for the table-read bus error split; the key of the token comes from the entropy source or the platform at reset; the trap bits set by default for a guest are `TLBIA`, `TLBSYNC`, `ICINV`, `WFE` and `SEV`; `RTT` takes the mask like `RTI`. *Also:* an example of atomics (compare-and-swap, `LL`/`SC`) and of the memory model (sequential consistency, a message-passing example) is added to the text. *Not done, stated plainly:* none of these is implemented; the text is not yet in the manual.
212. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R535): the final milestone is self-hosting, shown as a fixpoint.** The owner's words are in R535. *Decided:* the milestones, in order, are the assembler, linker, compiler back ends (LLVM, GCC, pcc), the emulator, the ports of OpenBSD and Debian to the point where one can log in over `ssh` to the QEMU backend, and then **self-hosting**: the system compiles its own tree with its own toolchain, reboots into the result, and works. *The fixpoint,* in the manner of the owner's imp72 project (whose `docs/BOOTSTRAP.md` has a seed that builds stage 1, stage 1 that builds stage 2 from the same sources, and the check that the two produce byte-identical output): a compiler built by a compiler built from the same sources produces the same bytes as the one that built it; the pdp64 check is the same, run on the pdp64 itself. *Consequences recorded for later:* a compiler and a tree whose output does not depend on the host (reproducible builds), the assembler, linker and compilers all running natively on the pdp64, and a test that compares the stages. *Not done, stated plainly:* none of it exists yet; nothing in the manual changes, because the manual describes the chip, not the milestones of its ports.
213. **DECISION (the assistant's, on the owner's remark of 2026-11-25; R536): the `_DB` forms of the atomic words are removed.** The owner asked what `db` is for ("i assumed it was nasm/masm syntax for data byte"). In this instruction set it meant a data-barrier variant that folded a fence into an atomic, from the time the memory model was weaker. The memory model is sequential consistency (kept by the decision on F15), so every atomic is already ordered, and the executor and the QEMU model give the `_DB` forms no behaviour of their own: they are 20 words that duplicate the plain ones. Judged by Occam (do not multiply entities without benefit), by one binary on every implementation (no word whose meaning differs between machines) and by the owner's principle of a coherent set kept in the head, the forms are removed: the atomic words become 24 (ten operations, `LL` and `SC`, each in the 8-byte and the 4-byte size). *The owner may overrule this.* *Not done at the time of writing:* the grammar, assembler, executor, tests and manual still have the `_DB` forms until the removal is made; the encodings freed are not yet reused.
214. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R537): the mnemonic names the operation, not the length of its encoding.** The owner's words are in R537. *Decided:* where the operands make the choice obvious, one mnemonic is written and the assembler picks the encoding (two, four, six bytes or longer); there are not separate mnemonics for the short and the long encodings of one operation. *What is not affected:* a suffix that names the size of the operand (the `L` of `XADDL`, the `Q` and `L` of `MOVQ` and `MOVL`) or a different operation is not an encoding length and stays; the rule is about spellings whose only difference is how many bytes the instruction takes. The survey of which mnemonics are affected, the assembler's choice rule, and the way a test forces a particular encoding are made by the assistant and reported to the owner. *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is changed yet.
215. **DECISIONS AND INSTRUCTIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R538): answers03.txt.** The owner's words are in R538. *Buildabook (the owner's tool; the assistant is to implement them in `../buildabook`):* the section sigil is a setting, a prefix that may be empty (an empty one gives `6.14.9`, a non-empty one gives `Section` and the like); appendices and every other top-level unit are parents of sections; a resolve-only Markdown mode; the exit status follows the Unix standards and the exit codes are documented in the manual page; an escape for `{{ }}` (C style `\{` or the old roff style is the owner's suggestion: the assistant chooses); sections that stand out of outline order are reordered in the output to match the outline and a warning is written; figures have counters of their own; the numbered-list ordinal question (the owner asks whether it already exists: to be checked); volumes, so that one outline covers a whole set and one run resolves every cross-reference across volumes (volume, chapter, section, page). *Sequential consistency:* the owner accepts losing up to 40% of speed for it, suspects that it will not be needed, and would be ecstatic at 2% from heavy techniques; asks for the gates as power draw (simulate or calculate comparables for the pdp64 design), for benches with `sim64` or `qemu-pdp64`, what TSO is, why MIPS abandoned the R10000 method (or whether it did), the documents opened and read properly (Lamport 1979, the PA-RISC and IBM manuals, Ranganathan and others, the SPARC V9 and Alpha facts verified), and whether sequential consistency improves safety and correctness (the 10 to 15% Oracle slowdown is very tolerable if it buys safety, closes classes of bugs and reduces the instruction set). TSO as the fallback is accepted. *Opcode errors:* no replacement is named in the error for a removed opcode (before 1.0 it is as if it never existed); the hints for the removed atomic `db` forms, `trapl` and `movql` are removed. *Accepted:* `jmp label` merged into `br`; `rori` dropped (`ror` takes a register or a constant); `MovImmBits` pinned. *A principle, added to the list:* speed is operations per cycle; reduce instruction counts, then byte counts, but always in service of fewer clock cycles; fewer bytes mean less fetch and fewer instructions (constructed right) mean fewer cycles; never add clock cycles to one instruction unless it saves a lot of fetch cycles, and the disparity with fetch is so great that this is unlikely. *Asked:* when the prefix encoding is run again it encodes compactly, so what is the second trap slot? (answered in conversation: a leftover of hand placement; the optimizer is to be re-run). *Not done, stated plainly:* none of it is implemented yet.
216. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R539): sequential consistency stays; the heavy technique is adopted for out-of-order cores if it holds up.** The owner's words are in R539, on `docs/encoding-work/why-not-sequential-consistency.md`. *Decided:* (1) sequential consistency is the architecture; the fallback if the margin of that document cannot be kept is TSO (errata 215); (2) the first implementation is an in-order core with a store buffer and ownership prefetch and no speculation (the bench in `docs/encoding-work/bench/sc/`: 0.9% to 9.3% slower than a relaxed model for a store miss of 10 to 100 cycles); (3) for out-of-order cores the heavy technique (post-retirement speculation with a checkpoint, in the manner of InvisiFence) is worth its power (+2% to 4% by the estimate, good to a factor of three) for the benefit (within about 2% of the most relaxed models in the published simulation), *if it holds up*: it is to be measured on the pdp64's own design before it is built, and the R10000 method (loads run early and are replayed on a snoop) is the first step. *Not done, stated plainly:* no out-of-order core exists; the measurements that would show whether the technique holds up (a real out-of-order model, many-core coherence) do not exist yet.
