217. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R540): the removes and merges of the instruction pass are taken.** The owner's words are in R540; the reasoning, the counts and the alternatives of each are in `docs/encoding-work/instruction-pass.md`. *Removed:* C1 the deferred-fault loads `MOVx.D` and `MOVx.UD` (14 words); C2 `TRAPCC.cc` (14 words); C3 `CBPL`, `CBMI`, `CBVC`, `CBVS`; C4 `MTDMW` and the windows `DMW0` and `DMW1`; C5 the alias `WAIT` and the placeholder words `SYSPG0` to `SYSPG5`. *Merged:* C6 `MFCPUID`, `MTASID` and `MFASID` into `MFSR` and `MTSR`; C7 `FMSUB` and `FNMADD` into `FMADD` and `FNMSUB` (with an exact `FNEG`); C8 the `binary16` arithmetic into conversion-only (the six conversions and the `binary32` operation; the pass noted a cost of two instructions per half-precision operation); C9 `STUA`/`CLUA` and the `UA` status bit into the one mechanism `.U` (the pass noted the block copy as the case to check); C10 the explicit unaligned moves `MOVUB`, `MOVUW`, `MOVUL`, `MOVUQ` into plain loads and stores, with a new hardware guarantee that plain accesses work at any address (the atomics still fault when misaligned: errata 211). *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is implemented yet; the measurement items of the pass (C11 to C17: the flags class, the three-register ALU, the probes, the absolute mode, the tag branches, the select words, the vector family) stay open until measured.
218. **DECISIONS AND A PRINCIPLE (the owner's, 2026-11-25; R541).** The owner's words are in R541. *Decided:* (1) **a misaligned access to strongly ordered device-register memory is a precise fault;** normal memory and device weakly ordered memory allow misaligned accesses (errata 217), because a device register can have side effects on a read or a write and a split access would repeat them; this is consistent with the atomics, which fault on device memory; (2) **`ror` and `rol` are one operation by assembler mapping** (mnemonics are cheap: the assembler maps a rotate-left onto the rotate right that is there, with the count 64 minus n for a constant); the encodings of `rol` are reviewed so that no short form is lost. *A principle, added to the list:* **precedent is neutral.** That another architecture does something is not a pro; history and precedent are a source of ideas, not a way to rank them. Options are judged on the principles, and an idea found in precedent is judged like any other idea. Added the same day (the owner: \"consistency of the arch itself is more important than duplicating other arches\"): the consistency of the architecture itself outranks matching other architectures. *Not done, stated plainly:* neither decision is implemented yet.
219. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-26; R542): the four changes of the precedent re-read are taken.** The owner's words are in R542; the reasoning is in `docs/encoding-work/precedent-reread.md` (items B, D, F, G), under the principles of errata 218 (precedent is neutral; the consistency of the architecture outranks matching others). *Decided:* (B) the atomics write the destination last, like the rest of the machine (`XADD Rs, (Ra), Rd` and its family; the exact operand shapes are chosen by the assistant from the existing three-register leaf); (D) the memory-type codes are renumbered so that strictness is the number (cached 0, uncached 1, weakly ordered 2, strongly ordered 3), and the manual's and the decisions' words for type 3 agree (\"device, strongly ordered\", not write-combining); stricter wins is a maximum; (F) the exit codes and the exit vectors do not share numbers with different meanings; (G) the atomic mnemonics are renamed in the pdp64's own way (the machine's names BIS, BIC, XOR, AND and the size suffix rules), not RISC-V's amo names with an X. *The six re-judge items (IOMMU shape, atomic flags, hypervisor register names, the atomic word list, size suffix spellings, the CPUID name) are not decided by this.* *Done (2026-11-26):* (B) the atomics are written destination last: `AADD Rs, (Ra), Rd`, `CAS Rs, (Ra), Rd` (Rd is both the expected value going in and the old value coming out), `SC Rs, (Ra), Rd`, `LL (Ra), Rd`; the fields follow the operands (the destination is the lowest field, as the model of `tools/check-enc.py` lays every form out); (G) the fetch-and-operate words are the machine's own ALU names with an `A` in front: `AADD AAND ABIS AXOR AMAX AMIN AMAXU AMINU`, each with an `L` form for 4 bytes; `CAS`, `SWAP`, `LL`, `SC` keep their names; the set of operations is unchanged (item H is not decided here); (D) the memory types are cached 0, uncached 1, device weakly ordered 2, device strongly ordered 3 (`MT_DEVICE_WEAK` 2, `MT_DEVICE` 3), the stricter of the stages and the platform map is the largest code, and errata 218 says device weakly ordered where it said write-combining; (F) the exit codes are 0 an event taken at H = 1, 1 machine check (vector 1), 2 to 16 the exits for an instruction (15 the breakpoint, as vector 15; 12 a hypervisor register or `GENTER` used by a guest), 21 physical interrupt, 22 hypercall, 23 to 26 the stage-2 faults (invalid, refused, no device, outside the arena), and no code is 17 to 20, the hypervisor's own vectors. Implemented in the grammar, the assembler, the shared executor, sim64, QEMU, the LLVM back end, the tests, the manual and the annex.
220. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-26; R543): the pdp64 has no flags.** The owner's words are in R543, on the experiment `docs/encoding-work/flags-experiment.md` (pcc with no flag-reading instruction for integer compares against the baseline, 622 files and 36 programs: +0.54% bytes, 0.04% fewer instructions executed, 0.2% to 0.9% fewer cycles on the model; the flag unit is 178 gates; ADC and SBC are emitted 0 times by either compiler). *Decided:* the N, Z, V and C condition codes are removed from the architecture (they were the PDP-11's, bits 3 to 0 of the status word). Programs compare with compare-and-branch (`cbCC`) and compare into a register; nothing reads a flag and no instruction writes one. *Staged:* (1) the replacements first: a floating-point compare-and-branch or compare-into-register (`FCMP` and its flag branches go), long forms for the unsigned conditions of the compare-and-branch (the unsigned conditions reach only about 1 to 6 KB where the signed ones reach 40 KB or more), a flag-free compare into a register in place of `SET`, and the carry idioms without a carry flag (`add` then an unsigned compare of the sum with an addend, or a compare into a register; `ADC` and `SBC` go); (2) then the removal: `CMP`, `TST`, `BIT` and `MCMP` as flag setters, the 14 flag branches, `SET` as it is, the flag-reading traps, the flag writes of every ALU instruction (`add sub neg inc dec mul abs min max and bis bic xor com asl asr clr` and the shifts), the four flag bits of the status word (`MFPS`, `MTPS`, exception entry, the hypervisor exit and the saved contexts), `fneg32` and `fabs32` then write nothing. pcc's no-flags back end (`docs/encoding-work/bench/flags/noflags-peep.patch`) becomes the compiler's own. *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is implemented yet; the experiment is a corpus of 622 of 1,104 files compiled by pcc and 36 programs run on the model, and the six large programs and the floating-point programs were not run.
221. **IMPLEMENTED (errata 217, 218; R540, R541): the misaligned fault on strongly ordered device memory, and `rol` as an assembler mapping.** (A) A plain load or store that is not aligned to its size takes the misalignment fault (vector 4, EVA the address, nothing performed) when a page it touches has memory type device (3); types 0, 1 and 2 make the access; an access across two pages is judged by the stricter; with translation off an address where a device answers is strongly ordered, an address nothing answers is the bus fault as before. QEMU only holds page types (`pdp64_device_strict`, `check_device_misaligned`); sim64 is flat and has no types. Tests `tests/qemu/mmu-misaligned.S` and `sec-device-split.S` (generator `tests/sim/gen_devmis.py`), corpus row SEC-M13. (B) `rol` is the word `"rol"@0x110` of the group `besc`, an alias of `ror`; the assembler complements the constant count (`as64/asmenc.c`, in `eplan`), `rol` with a register count is refused, and the three leaves of `rol` are gone. `ror` has a two-byte form: `ror #n, rd` takes the place of the old `rol #n, rd` in the leaf of `add`, `sub` and `and` (a signed count of four bits, -8 to 7, so a negative count rotates left; the leaf `shl shr sar` moved to the pattern `rol` had), and the assembler uses it for a register that is source and destination (a three-operand `rol` or `ror` with equal registers too). Rotate left by 1 to 8 and rotate right by 1 to 7 are two bytes; rotate left by 9 to 15 in the two-byte form is gone (four bytes). Free first parcels: 8297 before and after. Compiled corpus (LLVM, 176 files): 774,144 to 774,140 bytes, no rotate longer.
222. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-26; R544): the pdp64 is exclusively a 32-register machine.** The owner's words are in R544, said when asked whether pcc's `pdp64/r20` and 16-register builds should stay. *Decided:* there is one machine, with 32 registers and one encoding; the 16-register builds (`pdp64/16`, and `pdp64/r20`, the original revision 20 with registers r0 to r15) are removed, not kept as variants: the compiler's `-m` machine variants, the assembler's `-m` option and the machine recorded in the object's flags, the old encoding tables and the tools and tests that compare against them. History documents (the r20 papers in `docs/orig`, the errata, the register) stay as they are. *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is removed yet.
223. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-26; R545): operand widths are marks on the operand, written in bits.** The owner's words are in R545, on the assistant's proposal that the width be a mark on the operand and the mnemonic only the operation (about 37 mnemonics carried a size in the name: `MOVB`, `MOVW`, `MOVL`, `MOVZB`, `MOVZW`, `MOVZL`, the `L` of the atomics and `BSWAPL`, the `32` of the single-precision words and `ROR32`). *Decided:* the marks are `.8`, `.16`, `.32` and `.64` (bits, not byte, word, long and quad, which have meant different things on different machines); a memory operand carries the width of the access (`mov (r1).8, r2`), a floating-point value its precision on the register (`.32` single, `.64` double, `.16` half where a conversion names it), the default is 64 bits; the size suffixes of the mnemonics are removed (the owner, the same day: \"i choose u8 not 8u. in case of conflit, we can have .s8 also for signed\": the zero-extending marks are `.u8`, `.u16` and `.u32`; `.8`, `.16` and `.32` are signed by default and `.s8`, `.s16` and `.s32` are accepted as an explicit signed spelling where `.8` would be ambiguous; the assistant checks the assembler's lexer and the grammar for conflicts and shows the result to the owner); the assembler maps each marked operand onto the opcode that exists, so no encoding changes. This settles item I of the precedent re-read (the 4-byte form spelled three ways). *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is implemented yet.
224. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-26; R546): the system register number is 12 bits.** The owner's words are in R546, after the assistant found that the grammar (`form-MFSR = o1:reg5 o2:imm12u`) has a 12-bit system register number in the second parcel of `MFSR` and `MTSR` while the manual's statement said 8 bits. *Decided:* 12 bits, numbers 0 to 4095; the general registers are 5 bits (32 registers). The manual is corrected. *Open, not decided by this:* the expansion of `abs`, `min` and `max` (errata 98 left it open: the select they sit on is not defined).
225. **DECISION (the owner's, 2026-11-26; R547): `abs`, `min`, `minu`, `max` and `maxu` are expanded by the assembler over existing instructions.** The owner's words are in R547; errata 98 had made the five words assembler aliases and left the expansion open (the select they would sit on was never defined). *Decided:* the expansion is over what exists: the masks `MASKEQZ` and `MASKNEZ`, `setCC`, compare-and-branch (`cbCC`) and the ordinary ALU words (the assistant chooses the shortest and fastest correct sequence for each, with the scratch register that the ABI makes available to the assembler, and shows the measurements); no `csel` or other new opcode is added; the five native `AESC` sub-opcodes (`abs` 0x08, `min` 0x10, `minu` 0x11, `max` 0x12, `maxu` 0x13) are removed from the grammar, the executor, QEMU and the compilers' tables, and the manual's entries show the expansions. *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is implemented yet.
226. **DECISIONS (the owner's, 2026-11-26; R548): the six questions of the width-marks note are taken as the assistant proposed.** The owner's word is in R548, on `docs/encoding-work/width-marks-syntax.md` (60 words carry a width, precision, signedness or extension; the lexer finding: a dot is a name character, so `r1.8` is silently a symbol today). *Decided:* the marks `.8 .16 .32 .64`, `.u8 .u16 .u32` and `.s8 .s16 .s32` (errata 223) are written glued to the end of the operand, in every addressing mode; names stop before a mark and registers are recognized by character, so `r1.8` is a register with a mark and `buf.8` must be written quoted; (A) the `.U` user qualifier of the seven user-memory moves stays a mnemonic suffix; (B) names that are different operations stay (`AMAXU`, `AMINU`, `DIVU`, `MINU`, `EXTZB` and the like); (C) the 359 vector words keep their lane letters for now and move to the same marks in a second step; (D) `fcvt` names its integer side with `.s32`, `.u32`, `.s64` and `.u64`, and a plain register there is a 64-bit signed integer; (E) a width mark on the destination register of a load is refused (a register is always written in full); (F) a symbol that ends in a mark must be quoted. Side finding to be fixed with it: `r5-8` is documented as a synonym of `-8(r5)` and is rejected today (`sp-8` reads as the symbol `sp` minus 8). *Not done, stated plainly:* nothing is implemented yet.
227. **DECISIONS AND A REFINEMENT (the owner's, 2026-11-26; R549): the width letters b, w, l, q are the notation again; the marks stay.** The owner's words are in R549. *The refinement of a principle:* precedent for the design of a thing is neutral (errata 218), but **for notation precedent matters**, by the principle of least astonishment: people have finger memory, and a familiar spelling is less to type and less to hold in the head; the letters `w` and `d` that the assistant called ambiguous are settled by the machine's heritage: it is a **16-bit-word machine** in the PDP-11's sense, so `b` is 8 bits, `w` 16, `l` 32 and `q` 64 (the VAX's and the Alpha's letters; `d` is not a width letter for integers). *Decided:* (1) the integer words take the letters as mnemonic suffixes, as before the marks (`movb`, `movw`, `movl`, `movq`, `movzb`, `movzw`, `movzl`, `bswapw`, `bswapl`, the atomics with `l`, `rorl`), and the disassembler and the compilers print the letters; (2) the marks of errata 223 and 226 (`.8 .16 .32 .64`, `.u8 .u16 .u32`, `.s8 .s16 .s32`) stay as accepted spellings and are the way to say signedness, zero extension and a floating-point precision, but an operand is not marked unless there is a reason; (3) the README says, in its opening, that a word is 16 bits, a long 32 and a quad 64. *Open:* the floating-point precision spelling (the marks `.32` and `.64` stay unless the owner prefers the PDP-11's `f` and `d` letters) and the data model (the assistant's answer to the owner's question on LP64 against LLP64 is in the conversation: LP64 stays). *Not done, stated plainly:* the assembler, the disassembler and the compilers do not yet accept and print the letters.
